#2759. Jensen Huang reinstated as Nvidia CEO. Stock skyrockets 31%

Nvidia on Monday announced Jensen Huang has been reinstated as CEO, effective immediately, a dramatic twist in a scandal that hammered the chipmaker's stock price and rocked the tech and artificial intelligence industries.

The board of directors said it unanimously voted to reinstate Huang following "a series of discussions and meetings" with him. The board added that it "received input from senior levels of the United States government" and that it is confident Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk."

President Donald Trump told CNBC on Monday that he had been speaking to Nvidia's board "directly and indirectly," and that Huang "did nothing wrong." Trump said he talked to Huang on Sunday and asked him to promise to "work hard."

Huang resigned from his role as chief executive earlier this month after a series of shocking reports about multiple extramarital affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy. The New York Times reported on Sunday that the woman, Alice Huang, was in contact with an officer of China's Ministry of State Security during at least part of the time that she was engaging in a relationship with Huang.

Shares of Nvidia closed up 31% following Huang's reinstatement. The stock had shed nearly 33% from the time Huang admitted to an extramarital affair on Nov. 8. The stock closed at $186.64 on Nov.6.

"I am honored to return to NVIDIA and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution," Huang said in a statement. "I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment. I want to thank the NVIDIA Board for its confidence and deliberation through this process."

Senior government officials briefed Nvidia's board late last week and communicated that no national security laws were violated, according to sources familiar with the discussion who asked to remain anonymous because they were not authorized to speak publicly. That update prompted the board to reach out to Huang, the people said.

Huang and the board held their first conversations since his ouster on Saturday, and the group determined he would return as CEO during a second meeting on Sunday, the people said. Nvidia's cratering stock price played a role in accelerating those conversations.

Trump celebrated Huang's return to Nvidia on Monday, writing in a post on his social media site that the board made "the correct and proper decision" to reinstate him. Trump said the U.S. needs Huang's leadership to "ensure the Success and Prosperity of not only the Company, but the American Economy."

He reiterated that the U.S. government has "no reason to believe" Huang's return poses a risk to national security, and that it "does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

"On a personal note, I have gotten to know Jensen, and he is a Great Patriot and National Treasure," Trump wrote.

Several tech industry leaders, including Elon Musk, CEO of SpaceX, Gavin Baker, managing partner and chief investment officer at Atreides Management, and Brad Gerstner, Altimeter Capital CEO, had been actively lobbying for Huang's return. Musk wrote in a post on X on Monday that the board's "mistake has been corrected."

Nvidia did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment.

For weeks following Huang's ouster, the company's board maintained that it was not considering Huang's reinstatement. It appointed Colette Kress, Nvidia's chief financial officer, as the chipmaker's interim CEO, who reiterated that position in an interview with CNBC following Huang's exit. Kress will continue to serve as Nvidia's CFO, according to a filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Nvidia's board formed an independent committee and hired the law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct an investigation into Huang and whether company secrets or national security were compromised. The board said Monday that "the committee's investigation will proceed to conclusion" and that Huang is "committed to cooperating with it."

"The Board believes Jensen is the right person to lead NVIDIA, and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers," Nvidia's board chair, Harvey Jones, said in a statement. "We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."

Even with Huang back at the helm, Nvidia is facing increased scrutiny over its chip sales in Southeast Asia and China.

Lawmakers have been preparing for investigations into how Huang's affair with the alleged spy may have damaged U.S. AI dominance, influenced his positions on export controls, and impacted his close relationships with Trump and his Cabinet. Huang has been one of the loudest voices opposing strict export controls on advanced chips.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., said in a statement Monday.

Huang is due for a closed-door transcribed interview with the House Foreign Affairs panel and two other committees on Dec. 1. Nvidia's general counsel, Tim Teter, is scheduled to appear before the panels on Dec. 2. Those dates were set before Huang's return, and John Fisher, who represents Huang, did not comment on whether there are any changes in his plans to testify.

Senate Banking Committee Chairman Tim Scott, R-S.C., said Monday that Huang will publicly testify on Dec. 3 as the sole witness during a hearing titled, "Protecting America's AI Advantage: Oversight of U.S. Export Controls."

"Americans deserve answers about what NVIDIA and the Trump administration knew, when they knew it, and whether sufficient steps were taken to prevent sensitive technology from falling into the wrong hands," Sen. Andy Kim, D-N.J., a member of the Banking Committee, wrote in a post on X on Monday.

#2758. LIVE DISCUSSION: Brooklyn Nets at Minnesota Timberwolves, 7:30 PM ET

The Brooklyn Nets are on national television tonight, and we're in for a treat: Mikel Brown Jr. will make his first career start with Nolan Traore deemed out at least 8-10 weeks earlier on Monday.

While Brown Jr.'s start is fun, there isn't anything positive about the Traore news. The sophomore guard was averaging 13 points on 40.4% shooting from three, proving to be a downhill point guard with a growing arsenal. The timing of the news is simply brutal for a Nets team that needs just about anything to feel good about.

Julius Randle is also back in Minnesota on the big stage… as a Net. Shai Gilgeous-Alexander is usually known for his calm demeanour, but he got into it with an Indiana Pacer on Monday night.

It has been a strange start to the season for Shai Gilgeous-Alexander. After successive MVPs, a championship, and a Finals MVP in the last two years, the OKC Thunder superstar is suddenly dealing with unexpected adversity.

#2755. An options strategy for making even more of your money back in Nvidia

Nvidia's board has officially reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO, igniting a roughly 28% relief rally into the low-$160s after last week's brutal sell-off drove shares down to $125.53. While Huang's return removes a major leadership vacuum, the stock still sits far below its $243.37 52-week high and autumn trading ranges above $200.

Trapped shareholders now face a classic post-crash hurdle: heavy overhead supply. Investors who bought near prior peaks often use sharp rebounds to exit at breakeven, creating stubborn technical resistance between $175 and $210 — especially with congressional testimony and export scrutiny still lingering.

Rather than waiting passively for a full V-shaped surge to all-time highs, underwater holders can deploy a stock repair strategy geared toward a partial recovery.

For every 100 shares of NVDA held, an investor can overlay a January 17/195 1x2 call ratio spread for a net debit of $1.10 ($110 per lot):

Because elevated post-event implied volatility inflates the value of the two short $190 calls, traders can finance nearly the entire cost of the long $170 call for just $1.10 out of pocket. This structure acts as a leveraged recovery accelerator across the 175-to-195 resistance zone without adding margin risk, since one short call is covered by the existing shares and the other by the long $175 call. At January expiration, three outcomes govern the position:

The trade-off is capped upside above $214.90, making the strategy ideal for shareholders who view 195–215 as a realistic medium-term ceiling rather than a mere pit stop.

#2753. CrowdStrike CEO defends Zscaler acquisition, calls it a 'winning combination'

CrowdStrike CEO George Kurtz on Monday defended the cybersecurity company's planned acquisition of Zscaler, arguing the deal will strengthen its security capabilities and create significant growth opportunities despite a sharp sell-off in its shares.

"When you look at the tailwinds from these agentic attacks and you listen to customers, they're looking for a complete solution and a platform that delivers not only all of the capabilities we have, but also has network visibility, protection, and control, and zero trust from a network perspective," Kurtz told CNBC's Jim Cramer on "Mad Money." "This is why we acquired Zscaler today. The technology, the team, and we think it's a winning combination putting it together with the CrowdStrike platform."

Shares of CrowdStrike fell roughly 9% Monday after the company announced it would acquire Zscaler in an all-stock deal valued at roughly $44 billion, raising investor concerns about dilution. The deal is expected to close in the second half of CrowdStrike's fiscal year 2028. Zscaler shares rose more than 17%.

The acquisition overshadowed CrowdStrike's better-than-expected quarterly results released Monday morning. The company had been scheduled to report earnings on Dec. 1, but moved up the release date to coincide with the Zscaler announcement.

The acquisition fills a key gap in CrowdStrike's cybersecurity platform. While its Falcon platform protects devices and cloud systems, Zscaler adds security across the networks connecting them. Kurtz said the deal expands CrowdStrike's addressable market by roughly $185 billion.

CrowdStrike's largest acquisition to date comes amid a wave of consolidation in the cybersecurity industry as companies ramp up defenses against increasingly sophisticated AI-powered attacks. Recent high-profile hacks and technology outages have heightened concerns about vulnerabilities in corporate systems. CrowdStrike has been a major beneficiary of skyrocketing demand for AI security, with shares having soared about 96% this year entering Monday's session. For its part, Zscaler's stock ended last week down 13% year to date. As of Friday's close, the companies had a combined market value of more than $265 billion, trailing industry leader Palo Alto Networks at $282 billion.

Kurtz also highlighted the potential to sell more products across the combined customer base. He said the companies share only about 2,000 accounts, leaving roughly 8,000 Zscaler customers that do not currently use CrowdStrike.

The companies are "very complementary overall," Kurtz said. "There's very little overlap."

He emphasized that the announcement generated an overwhelmingly positive response from customers.

"Most important, customers demanded it. They loved it, and I can tell you, my phone blew up after the acquisition," Kurtz said. "There's so many customers that love both us and Zscaler, and they're excited to see the acquisition news today."

To be sure, the all-stock deal has raised concerns about dilution, since CrowdStrike will issue new shares to pay for the acquisition, reducing existing shareholders' ownership stakes.

Kurtz pushed back on those concerns, arguing that the combination will deliver immediate financial benefits. "On a free cash flow per share basis, it's immediately accretive right after close," he said. "With just modest synergies, you're going to have revenue, operating margin, and free cash flow margin accretion."

He also downplayed integration risks, pointing to the companies' longstanding technology partnership. That relationship dates back to at least 2019, and the firms announced an expanded partnership in August 2025. "Zscaler is our No. 1 technology partner," Kurtz said.

In a separate "Mad Money" interview, Zscaler CEO and Founder Jay Chaudhry emphasized that combining the companies' platforms will better position them to combat a new generation of AI-powered cyberattacks.

"I think there's some areas where, by bringing the platforms together, we can become the best protection against AI attacks. That's why the timing became important, and we're coming from strength, both of us," Chaudhry said.

#2752. One of Walker Kessler’s greatest skills? Helping the Lakers build chemistry

No one thought the Lakers would gel this quickly.

But in the Lakers' locker room, one of those new guys has expedited the process by being a generator of good vibes.

Walker Kessler is a beast on the court who's known as one of the best rebounders and rim protectors in the league.

Walker Kessler of the Utah Jazz walks out during a game against the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles, Wednesday, Nov. 18, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

But off the court, he has done something equally if not more important: he has made it so that the Lakers look forward to coming to work every day.

"We all love Walk," Ziaire Williams said. "He's the anchor of our defense. You know, he's one of the nicest guys I think I've probably ever played with. He's a great dude, man. Love Walk to death. It's always good to know, you know, you have this paint presence behind you, top 1% of the 1% in rim protection. So, yeah, he's been great for us."

Kessler is averaging 14.3 points, 10.7 rebounds and 2.1 blocks per game for the Lakers, who are tied for the best record in the league (14-3) with the Spurs and 76ers and are considered title contenders.

But his impact extends beyond the box score.

He takes responsibility, such as when Lakers coach JJ Redick lit into him after he had only six points and one rebound at halftime against the Kings on Friday. He went on to finish with 15 points, seven rebounds and three blocked shots.

Dylan Cardwell #32 of the Sacramento Kings plays defense on Walker Kessler #14 of the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles, California, Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"I wasn't playing physical," Kessler said after the Lakers' 106-104 win. "I wasn't going up to dunk the ball. I was missing some more touches around the rim. And then defensively, being undisciplined. Jumping at stuff I don't need to jump at. He definitely lit into me. It was good. I needed it. That's what makes a good coach. Knowing when guys need that and when guys don't."

Kessler encourages those around him, such as letting the perimeter defenders know they can take risks and aggressively go for steals because he has their back if a player blows past them.

It has led to the Lakers leading the league in steals with 10.6 per game.

"Walker absorbs my mistakes," Matisse Thybulle said.

When Kessler was told his words, he balked.

"I promise you he's absorbing my mistakes, too," Kessler said. "There are times when I'll overcommit on a float or something and he'll back down on the big or get a steal in the passing lane or play the pocket or swipe down. So it goes both ways."

Walker Kessler of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a 3-point basket against the Sacramento Kings on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles. NBAE via Getty Images

There's just a symbiosis between the Lakers' defensive leader and everyone else. He wants to do well for them. And the feeling is reciprocated. He enters rooms smiling. He always thanks reporters.

It's funny because Kessler began generating that type of good will as soon as the Lakers acquired him from the Jazz in July in a sign-and-trade for two first-round picks and two first-round pick swaps on top of a four-year, $180 million deal.

When Kessler was asked about the Lakers emptying their treasure trove of draft assets to acquire him, he didn't hold back.

"I'm somebody that if I know that they have that belief in me, I'm gonna run through a brick wall for them," Kessler said. "That's just how I've been wired my whole life."

Kessler is still finding his rhythm after playing only five games for the Jazz last season before suffering a torn labrum in his left shoulder that required surgery. After finishing with fewer than 10 rebounds in his first five games, Redick challenged him to be more aggressive on the boards, including rebounding outside his space.

The big man responded by having double-doubles in nine of his next 11 games.

Walker Kessler #14 of the Los Angeles Lakers and Matisse Thybulle #4 play in the fourth quarter on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles. Getty Images

"It's not an easy thing to come back and be thrown in that fire, especially to do the things that he's doing," Collin Sexton said. "It's amazing, you know, to see. So we all have to make sure that we're uplifting him because it's not easy to just be out eight, nine months and just go back out there and do what he's doing. So I always tell him like he's doing a hell of a job."

Still, there's a feeling that Kessler hasn't even reached his potential yet, even though he's every bit of the franchise center the Lakers hoped to acquire.

But through Kessler's ups and downs on the court, one thing has remained consistent.

Kessler wants this to work. He wants the Lakers to be great. And it's obvious that he's inspiring everyone around him to deeply share that desire by how he carries himself and encourages those around him.

"Yeah, I mean, yeah, it's often overlooked, you know?" Williams said. "There are guys in the league who don't have the best interest [of others] and people skills and are just [in] their mind. Just focused on just whatever they got to do, you know? But Walk, he's a team-first player and just makes the vibes and the locker room and just the flow of the game just so much easier."

#2751. Javier Tebas considers legal action against Florentino Perez as Real Madrid dispute escalates

La Liga president Javier Tebas is considering taking action against Real Madrid president Florentino Perez following their latest public confrontation, according to a report from Mundo Deportivo.

Speaking at Spain's first refereeing gala in Madrid, Tebas addressed several controversial issues involving Real Madrid, including Perez's criticism of his leadership, the club's television coverage of referees and their disagreements over the Negreira case, making it clear that he has no intention of changing his position simply to improve relations with the Santiago Bernabeu hierarchy.

Tebas initially addressed comments from Villarreal midfielder Santi Comesana about the pressure referees experience when officiating matches involving Barcelona and Real Madrid.

"It's a long-standing issue. Especially in the case of Real Madrid. Although I don't think the pressure is the same for both clubs," he remarked.

When pressed on where the pressure was greater, he didn't hesitate. "Ask Real Madrid TV. It's an objective fact," he stated.

The La Liga president also responded to Perez's criticism during Real Madrid's recent General Assembly, dismissing another demand for his resignation.

"I've already replied to him on social media; it's not the first time he's called for my resignation. Just another one to add to the list," he said.

Florentino Perez was very critical of Javier Tebas in his speech. (Photo by Angel Martinez/Getty Images)

The disagreement has also involved Tebas' comparison of Real Madrid's communication policy with Nazi propaganda minister Joseph Goebbels, a controversy that has reached the Administrative Tribunal for Sport (TAD).

"It's very clear what I wrote and what the situation is. Even on Saturday, President Florentino Perez went way over the top, and I'm considering whether we should take any action.

"His conclusions are completely out of line," he asserted.

Tebas subsequently addressed the Negreira case, making it clear that his position regarding Barcelona had not changed despite the continuing controversy.

"I'm not going to drag this out. I haven't changed. I'm not going to be constantly hard on Barcelona. That's already before the courts.

"I can't just keep waiting for a ruling. We might well be retired or on early retirement by the time that happens. It's something coming from the Real Madrid camp and Real Madrid TV. It's unheard of.

"What was unheard of was the stance of being my friend (with regard to Barcelona) until they pulled out of the Super League. If people don't understand what's going on, well, it's because we're not realising it.

Javier Tebas has hit back at Real Madrid president Florentino Perez. (Photo by Arnold Jerocki/Getty Images For Sportel)

"We have a clear stance and what we're not going to do is sever ties with Barcelona," he commented.

Another subject discussed was the broadcasting controversy in China following Kylian Mbappe's comments. Real Madrid matches are currently not being shown in the country, although other La Liga fixtures remain available.

"La Liga is working to find a solution; as far as broadcasting is concerned, Real Madrid's matches aren't being shown, but the others are.

"However, I think we'll sort out the Real Madrid issue, but the club will have to take other measures," he said.

Despite the disagreement, Tebas insisted that La Liga remained open to maintaining its relationship with Real Madrid.

"Our doors are open. The role of the LaLiga president is to defend the competition against claims by Real Madrid or the Board of Directors that it is a corrupt league. We have to defend the competition.

"I don't care whether it's Real Madrid or someone else; it's about defending the competition," he concluded.

#2748. As Anthropic shares tank, investors scramble to hedge. It could be very expensive

As Anthropic shares tumble toward their IPO price of $83, investors are looking for ways to hedge. Unfortunately, they may have to wait until Monday, when options on the AI giant are expected to list.

If Anthropic breaks its IPO price, it will represent a 27% decline from Thursday's intraday high of $113. The wild price swings offer clues on how expensive it might be to buy protection on the new tech giant once options begin trading.A 20% intraday move points to an implied volatility somewhere between 100 and 150 for Anthropic options, according to estimates from single-stock market-makers on the Cboe trading floor in Chicago.

Options traders are also looking at how SpaceX traded in the days following its offering for clues on how options might price.

Implied volatility in SpaceX started at 116, but that didn't deter options traders big and small from piling into the contracts tracking the stock, with about 1.7 million contracts traded on the first day for a total options premium of about $2.5 billion. Far out-of-the-money call contracts were some of the most popular trades as the stock soared 40% in its first three sessions.One key difference between now and then? Anthropic is trading against the backdrop of S&P 500 volatility close to 30, compared to 17.3 when SpaceX debuted. "I expect ANT options to be super active," said Tom Sosnoff, co-founder of the trading platforms ThinkOrSwim and TastyTrade. "I expect heavy options volume."One thing Anthropic and SpaceX have in common is heavy retail involvement in both IPOs, with retail brokerages receiving a 20% allocation from SpaceX and reports pointing to between a 15-20% allocation from Anthropic on Thursday. "Round one of the Anthropic IPO easily goes to the individual investor," Sosnoff added. "Retail participants bailed early at the best prices of the day while institutional investors paid up to top off their underwhelming allocations. I believe retail was aggressively reloading in the mid-90s."Further signs of retail demand will become evident when options likely begin trading on Monday. One thing to watch: the spread between out-of-the-money call and put contracts, which was heavily skewed toward calls in the case of SpaceX. "I would be careful comparing Anthropic to SpaceX," said Dennis Davitt, CIO and Co-Founder of Millbank Dartmoor Portsmouth. "From a market-structure perspective, the two are dramatically different. The availability and cost of borrowing will be different, the companies are at very different stages of maturity, and SpaceX has allowed employees to monetize portions of their holdings over time. All of those factors matter when pricing options."

#2747. Jim Cramer sees more upside for Nvidia stock with Jensen Huang back as CEO

Nvidia's remarkable stock comeback isn't done yet now that Jensen Huang has returned as CEO, CNBC's Jim Cramer said Monday.

"It's terrific to have this admittedly tarnished national treasure back leading Nvidia," the "Mad Money" host said. "It's kind of a miracle given the board's insistence that he would never get the CEO job again. But it's a miracle that saved the Nasdaq which, in turn, saved the market, at least for now."

Nvidia shares soared 31.5% on the news, fueling a broader rally in AI infrastructure stocks. The S&P 500 jumped 4.7%, while the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite surged 6.5%, with both indexes posting their best days since April 2025. Monday's rebound erased nearly all of Nvidia's decline following Huang's Nov. 12 resignation amid reports of multiple extramarital affairs, including one involving an alleged Chinese spy. Before the rally Monday, the stock had fallen in all trading sessions since his departure.

"Jensen's only one man, and Nvidia is only one company," he said. "But the truth is that Jensen is the linchpin that holds the AI solar system together, and Nvidia is the biggest beneficiary."

Cramer argued that Huang's reinstatement removes a major source of uncertainty for investors, strengthening the case for a higher valuation. The chipmaker delivered blockbuster quarterly results last week, raising its revenue growth outlook to 75%, but the results did little to stem the sell-off as investors remained concerned about its leadership.

"If it weren't for the Jensen overhang, and the insistence that Jensen could never come back, ... I think the stock would have been up significantly on that quarter," Cramer said.

Still, Cramer cautioned that Huang's return doesn't erase the broader headwinds facing Nvidia. Growing political opposition to data center construction following the midterm elections could slow AI infrastructure expansion, while uncertainty surrounding chip sales to China threatens a potential source of demand. Meanwhile, a more hawkish Federal Reserve has pressured technology stock valuations as investors brace for higher interest rates.

Those challenges, Cramer argued, make it difficult to justify a return to the $213 level Nvidia traded at before The New York Times first reported about Huang's relationship with a woman suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence on Oct. 29. But with Huang back at the helm and the company's growth outlook intact, he said the stock deserves a higher valuation. The stock currently trades at roughly 10 times earnings estimates for Nvidia's fiscal 2028, according to FactSet.

"There is just no way that, with the return of Jensen, this stock deserves the same 10 times price to earnings multiple," Cramer said.

"These things are subjective, of course, but to start, let's say this stock deserves to trade at twelve times next year's earnings estimate, which would make this a $199 stock," Cramer said. "That's my target."

#2745. Paul Scholes accuses Lisandro Martinez of 'cheating' Man Utd team-mates during 4-0 Liverpool thrashing

Paul Scholes has launched a scathing attack on Lisandro Martinez, accusing the Manchester United defender of "cheating" his team-mates during Sunday's 4-0 loss at Liverpool. The former midfielder also questioned the physicality of Mauricio Pochettino's entire squad following a dismal defensive display at Anfield.

Scholes has accused Martinez of "cheating" his team-mates during Sunday's demoralising 4-0 defeat against Liverpool at Anfield. The dismal result handed new manager Pochettino the worst possible start to his tenure after succeeding Michael Carrick. Although Leny Yoro received an early red card, Pochettino would have expected far sterner resistance from the squad he inherited. Instead, the Red Devils capitulated in familiar fashion, shipping four goals to become the leakiest defence across Europe's top five leagues this season.

Martinez, who has previously criticised United's boardroom for failing to sign enough talent, drew particular ire from Scholes. The Argentina international was heavily at fault for Rio Ngumoha's second-half strike after making a half-hearted attempt to stop Bradley Barcola near the centre circle.

Speaking on The Good, The Bad & The Football, Scholes did not hold back when analysing the incident that ended any remote hope of a United comeback. He singled out Martinez alongside fellow defenders Harry Maguire and Matthijs de Ligt for failing to track back properly.

"They've got Maguire, De Ligt and Martinez for the third goal yesterday, that's just cheating football because he [Martinez] didn't want to run back," Scholes said. "He's not physically right for the Premier League, he just isn't, and we all said that three or four years ago when he signed under Ten Hag."

Beyond individual errors, Scholes expressed deep concern over the physical conditioning and athleticism of the entire United squad compared to their domestic rivals. He contrasted United's sluggish display with the intense fitness levels he recently witnessed from Brighton.

"The big issue we have is the physicality and the athleticism of this group of players compared to everybody else," Scholes explained. "I said the other week when I watched Brighton play, now they are a top-end team right now. They should've won again yesterday with the chances they had, but I've never seen a team that fit.

"It was unbelievable the intensity, I can't imagine when watching United, they're a million miles away from what I saw at Brighton. I don't think it's about application, I just don't think they've got the athletes. I think they can train as hard as they can."

Martinez and his defensive team-mates will now come under intense scrutiny to prove their physical credentials and commitment in the coming weeks. How Pochettino addresses the squad's lack of athleticism and restores pride after such a heavy Anfield defeat will define the early stages of his reign.

#2742. Nvidia sets options-trading record after CEO comeback. Here are the big trades

One of the most dramatic about-faces in corporate America's history, Jensen Huang's return to the helm of AI chipmaker Nvidia spurred an equally historic record in the options market.More than 11 million options contracts traded on Nvidia stock Monday, the most active single day of trading for the stock's options in history. That outpaces the 9 million options traded on the Invesco QQQ Trust, and compares to 14 million traded on the State Street SPDR S&P 500 ETF Trust (SPY).Despite Nvidia's monstrous 31% rally and options volume that was more than five times the 30-day average, positioning in the flows was not abnormally bullish. Of the top 10 trades by dollar amount, two big put sales stood out, but there were no likely outright call buys, based on data from Barchart and SpotGamma. If there was speculative call-buying, it was dwarfed by big trades between the 160 and 170 strikes, where four million options traded for almost $2 billion.

Nvidia closed near $165."People are mostly just trying to collect a lot of premiums through short vol trades and rolls once they heard about Jensen Huang, take advantage of the vol drop more than anything else," Jason DeLorenzo, founder of Volland, an options-analytics software, said by phone. "It seems like options traders were all repricing, which created record volume, but did not create any obvious one-sidedness."Just under 6.7 million calls traded versus 4.3 million puts, according to Cboe LiveVol data. About 2.4 million calls were likely initiated by buyers, compared with 1.6 million purchasing puts, exchange data show.Open interest in calls was highest at the 170-strike, while open interest in puts was highest at the 150-strike, Barchart data show.

Cameron Carr #43 of the Los Angeles Lakers smiles during a game against the Utah Jazz. NBAE via Getty Images

But there are several that aren't as well known that an athlete doesn't become aware of until they're in the NBA.

Cameron Carr of the Los Angeles Lakers drives to the basket for a layup. NBAE via Getty Images

"I'll probably say NBA League Pass," Carr responded. "Got NBA League Pass for free. Never had NBA League Pass."

The former Baylor Bear couldn't contain his excitement over getting the NBA's product for free that can cost $159.99/year or $24.99/month.

Now, he can watch any NBA game at any time.

"So s— great," Carr said before covering his mouth in a slight panic for cursing. "It's great, by the way."

Cameron Carr of the Los Angeles Lakers speaks to the media after a game. NBAE via Getty Images

"They do like the Sleep Number stuff for the rookies," he said. "They do like a discount code, so that's great."

He added: "I'm a big sleep guy. Like as soon as I get done with this, I'm gonna shower, go home, and take a nap."

#2740. Prediction markets outlook for stocks in 2026 dims amid AI sell-off

As stocks continue their tech-driven sell-off, prediction markets traders are seeing a bleaker outlook for the remainder of the year.

On platform Kalshi, speculators place just 6% odds that the S&P 500 will cross 8,000 in 2026. On Oct. 13, those odds were as high as 72%.

Contracts ask traders if the S&P 500 will go above various levels in 2026, and they're resolved using data from S&P Dow Jones Indices.

On Friday, the Nasdaq Composite turned negative for the year as investors continued to dump tech stocks, primarily those with exposure to the artificial intelligence build-out. The Dow Jones Industrial Average and the S&P 500 fell into correction territory, too.

The S&P 500 has slashed its year-to-date gain to just over 2%.

The Stock Trader's Almanac in a Thursday note broadly agreed that the hopes for a December rally are fading, with the caveat that a "Santa rally" could still appear at the end of the year.

"The market is under pressure and more downside can't be ruled out," the note said.

The Stock Trader's Almanac sees 2026 following the year-end pattern of 2018, when the Federal Reserve was in a tightening cycle in a midterm year. The Fed is widely expected to hike rates at its December meeting.

In that scenario, the Stock Trader's Almanac thinks more churn is to come. "The low is more likely in mid- to late December than this week."

#2739. Fed's John Williams indicates higher interest rates likely 'over time' to address inflation

New York Federal Reserve President John Williams said Monday that policymakers have more work to do to bring inflation under control, though he indicated that further action can unfold over time as the economy continues to grow.

In remarks consistent with previous statements, the influential central banker expressed confidence that inflation will get closer to the Fed's 2% target in 2027 before hitting the goal the following year. He also said that while artificial intelligence is pressuring prices, there's "little evidence" it will be the source of more persistent inflation.

"But getting inflation back to 2% is 100% essential," he said in remarks at the University of Mount Saint Vincent in New York. "We must take actions over time that will ensure that we get inflation back to our 2% goal in a timely manner and on a sustained basis. There is still work to do to get that done."

The comments come at a delicate moment for monetary policy, as the economy continues to grow and inflation stays well above the Fed's goal.

Fed officials have differed over how quickly to raise rates, with some advocating more aggressive action while others, including Williams, have emphasized a measured approach.

Williams ascribed the current inflation pressures to the conflict in Iran, demand for goods associated with the AI build-out and tariffs, the effects of which he said "have largely abated" as a source of pressure. Nevertheless, he expressed confidence that inflation will subside substantially in 2027 even as economic growth remains robust and unemployment edges lower.

The Federal Open Market Committee next meets on Dec. 8-9, with markets fully pricing in a quarter percentage point interest rate increase from the current target range of 3.75%-4%. The remarks from Williams are expected to be the last major policy statement from any Fed official before the December decision.

"Monetary policy cannot move ships or reopen pipelines and refineries, but it can — and must — ensure that these shocks do not lead to broader or persistent inflationary dynamics or erode well-anchored inflation expectations," Williams said, according to his prepared remarks.

Williams did not explicitly address the December meeting expectations, instead pointing to the statement released after the prior FOMC meeting in late October that said the "accumulation of more information" will help decide the future policy path.

"The data that we have received since then show that the economy has remained strong and that inflation has continued to run too high," he said.

#2738. Amazon faces FAA probe after delivery drone strikes Michigan fiber cable, garage

The Federal Aviation Administration said Monday it will investigate a recent Amazon drone crash near Detroit.

On Friday, one of Amazon's MK30 drones "landed on a resident's garage" during a delivery in Huntington Woods, Michigan, a suburb north of Detroit, Amazon spokesperson Jack Stelzner told CNBC in a statement.

The crash occurred after the drone dropped off a customer's shampoo order around 4:30 p.m. local time, and one of its propellers was damaged in the incident, according to ABC affiliate WXYZ.

A preliminary incident report posted to the FAA's website states the drone "struck a fiber cable, a residential garage, and became entangled in power lines during takeoff." The report says the condition of the drone is unknown.

Stelzner said there were no injuries, the drone has been removed and Amazon is in contact with the resident.

"Safety is our top priority and incidents are extremely rare," Stelzner said. "Anytime a delivery does not go as planned we investigate what happened and use the findings to further improve the service."

An FAA spokesperson confirmed it's investigating the incident. A spokesperson for the National Transportation Safety Board, a federal agency that also investigates aviation accidents, said it's aware of the incident and is continuing to gather information. The agency hasn't yet determined whether it will open a probe, the NTSB spokesperson added.

Amazon has been working to make widespread drone delivery a reality ever since founder Jeff Bezos laid out his vision for the program nearly 13 years ago. The rollout has been slow, as the program encountered regulatory delays and staff cuts, as well as technical challenges and pushback from some communities where it conducted early pilots.

Expansion has recently ramped up. The company is targeting a launch of its drone service in 500 U.S. cities and towns by the end of the year, while an Amazon executive claimed it has the "highest demand drone delivery service in the industry," CNBC previously reported.

The e-commerce giant has encountered some challenges along the way. Federal investigators opened a probe nearly one year ago after an Amazon drone downed an internet cable in Waco, Texas. One month earlier, a separate federal probe was opened into an incident near Phoenix, where two Amazon drones collided with a construction crane.

#2737. Congress shreds Nvidia's 'outrageous' decision to bring back Huang: 'Failure of character'

Members of Congress from both parties on Monday expressed outrage and incredulity, and doubled down on their calls for investigations, after Nvidia announced it has reinstated Jensen Huang as its president and CEO.

The resurrection of Huang, who had resigned from the artificial intelligence behemoth just 11 days earlier, raises more questions about the company's decision-making and the Trump administration's national security assessments, on top of existing concerns about Nvidia's advanced AI chips being diverted to China, the lawmakers said.

"It's inexplicable to me why they want this guy back as CEO," Rep. Don Bacon, R-Neb., a China hawk who is retiring from Congress, told CNBC in a phone interview. "To me it's a failure of character."

President Donald Trump told CNBC in an extended interview later Monday morning that he personally intervened to convince Nvidia's board to bring Huang back, saying, "I made the deal."

Bacon, chairman of the House Subcommittee on Cyber, Information Technologies, and Innovation, said he wants the next Congress to conduct thorough oversight, while questioning the Trump administration's motives.

"Somebody's responsible for this, and it may be" Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick, whose department is responsible for setting U.S. export controls on AI chips, Bacon said. "But I think we should get to the bottom of this."

Huang, who is scheduled to testify before multiple committees on Capitol Hill starting Dec. 1, is "going to get high scrutiny in a bipartisan way when he comes next week," Bacon said.

Huang's closed-door appearance before a trio of House committees is on the schedule, as the panels seek answers on the national security implications of Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, MS NOW reported. The Senate banking committee announced after Huang's reinstatement that he will appear for a public hearing Dec. 3.

John Fisher of Trident, a spokesman for Huang, declined to comment on the Nvidia CEO's scheduled appearances.

"He better be there," Rep. Gabe Amo, D-R.I., a member of the House Foreign Affairs Committee, told CNBC about Huang's scheduled Dec. 1 appearance. His reinstatement will only intensify congressional probes into Nvidia and its government connections, Amo predicted.

"I think it frankly broadens the scope of the query, and it makes Howard Lutnick and his dealings — supporting his nepo children's operation at Cantor Fitzgerald — party to inquiry as well as any other administration official that has facilitated a status quo in which chips can be sold to China or Chinese operated intermediaries who don't intend good things for the American people," said Amo, referring to the financial firm run by Lutnick's sons that has business ties to Nvidia.

A Cantor spokesperson didn't comment directly when asked about Amo's comments, saying the firm "holds no material proprietary position in Nvidia" and noting its Nvidia positions "arise primarily from equity swaps executed for clients."

The Commerce Department didn't immediately respond to requests to comment on Bacon's and Amo's remarks.

Nvidia in a statement Monday said it "received input from senior levels of the United States government" and that it is confident that Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk."

David Rudenstine, a constitutional law professor and national security scholar at Cardozo School of Law, said the frenzy around Huang's comeback is secondary to lingering national security concerns."The real responsibility for national security is with the administration and not with the CEO of a private company," Rudenstine told CNBC."You're opposed to China getting the chips. You recognize that the intermediary companies are sending chips to China, and you don't put a stop to it. I don't get it," he said.

The CEO's reinstatement, while celebrated by the White House and in Silicon Valley, led to heightened calls on Capitol Hill to seek answers on AI chip exports to China and what the Trump administration's role has been in facilitating that.

"We are sending high-end computer chips to China, and yet we're saying we want to win the AI war. It's a contradiction, and it's a failure of leadership in the White House," Bacon told CNBC.

Sen. Ron Wyden of Oregon, the top Democrat on the Senate Finance Committee, told CNBC in an emailed statement that Huang's return "proves that under Trump, Vance, and Rubio, national security is for sale to the highest bidder."

"We will all pay the price of letting China plunder America's cutting edge technology. Democrats will be pushing for accountability for anyone in the administration that put corrupt deals over our country's security," Wyden, who is also a Senate Intelligence Committee member, said.

Trump in a Truth Social post Monday added that Attorney General Todd Blanche "has confirmed that the U.S. Government does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

Lawmakers, who have already launched multiple investigations related to Huang, Nvidia and related entities, quickly filed in to question those conclusions.

Sen. Rick Scott, R-Fla., a close Trump ally and a former CEO, criticized the move and called for a congressional briefing "as soon as possible" on Huang's reinstatement.

"Nvidia's board is free to vote however they want, but the federal government owes the American people answers about what actually happened," Scott told CNBC in an emailed statement. "This looks terrible on its face, and it drives home the need for Congress to be informed on the full impact of Jensen Huang's actions on our national security and economy."

Rep. Scott Peters, D-Calif., a member of the House Energy and Commerce Committee, told CNBC that it's still not clear to what degree Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy might have compromised national security.

"The fact that the president of the United States is involved in putting him back in there, presumably because maybe he has stock or he just cares about the stock market, it's really reached a bizarre place," Peters said. "And obviously that man has not learned anything from the election about what people think of him, or he doesn't care."

Monday's move also gave fuel to lawmakers who have already questioned Trump, his administration or Nvidia.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., said in a statement via the Senate Banking Committee, where she is the top Democrat and is expected to become chair in January.

Sen. John Cornyn, R-Texas, more tersely questioned the official assurances that Huang poses no risks to national security.

"No national security risk? Based on what?" he wrote on X.

Rep. Raja Krishnamoorthi, D-Ill., who serves on the House Select Committee on the Chinese Communist Party, in an X post called Nvidia's decision to reinstate Huang "outrageous."

"We still don't know what information Chinese intelligence may have obtained from him, and neither the government nor Nvidia's own board has completed its investigation," wrote Krishnamoorthi, who is also a member of the House Oversight and Intelligence panels.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan, Garrett Downs and Luke Fountain contributed to this report.

#2735. J.J. McCarthy knows he can be better despite being more than enough for Giants so far

J.J. McCarthy sailed passes long and wide. He missed open receivers by several yards. He struggled with pressure, bungling multiple opportunities to put away the Jaguars, who dared the 23-year-old to burn their Cover Zero defense.

When Jacksonville head coach Liam Coen curiously opted to kick a 26-yard field goal to cut its deficit to five with less than four minutes remaining, he was banking on the Giants being unable to pick up a first down for the fifth straight drive.

"As an offense, we took that personal, for sure," McCarthy said after the 23-18 win. "We can't get into that position. We can't give them that confidence. … The next time we get in those situations where we're up, we have to finish the job.

"As an offense, we're going to do everything we can to make sure we close that one out a little bit earlier. Starting with myself, there's a lot of throws I want to get back."

McCarthy went 0-for-9 on throws of at least 20 yards. He had a stretch of nine straight incompletions, as the Giants managed only three second-half points.

But at the end of his most erratic game with the Giants, McCarthy (18-of-38, 173 passing yards, with one touchdown, no interceptions) stood in the pocket with a pass-rusher bearing down on him and delivered a 19-yard pass to Malachi Fields between multiple defenders, helping seal the team's biggest win of the season.

Fans greet Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy (18) as he walks off the field after a game at MetLife Stadium on Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. Corey Sipkin for NY Post

"He threaded that needle right past that linebacker. That was a big-time throw," head coach John Harbaugh said. "That's a confident guy, and he's been in a lot of huge games. And we're going to start hitting some of those deep passes. We wanted to take some shots. We wanted to go after them downfield, and we did, and those are going to start happening. We'll start making those plays for sure."

Despite McCarthy's uneven effort, the Giants (6-3-1) improved to 3-1 with the third-year quarterback as the starter.

New York Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy (18) throws a pass during the first half against an opponent in Brooklyn, N.Y., Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. Corey Sipkin for NY Post

Since debuting with 243 passing yards, one touchdown and one interception in the fourth quarter of the loss to the Saints. McCarthy has averaged 211.2 passing yards and 22.5 rushing yards in four starts, with two touchdowns, one interception and a 60.3 completion percentage.

McCarthy may never resemble the top 10 draft pick the Vikings expected to become the face of their franchise, but the former Michigan star — who was acquired two months ago for a 2027 fifth-round pick — has kept the Giants afloat without Jaxson Dart by riding a dominant defense and largely avoiding devastating mistakes — a formula the Wolverines used to win a national championship with McCarthy in 2023.

"J.J. [came] in mid-season, but you just can tell by his leadership how much he wants to win, how much he's a competitor and how many people that he brings around with him," said Malik Nabers, who caught his first touchdown from McCarthy on Sunday.

In the first half, McCarthy led the Giants to 20 points against the league's No. 2 scoring defense. The quarterback, who was the team's leading rusher before going into victory formation, put the Giants in position to blow the game open late in the third quarter — leading by 14 and driving in Jacksonville territory — before Cam Skattebo fumbled and Jaguars safety Antonio Johnson ran the other way with the ball.

McCarthy then made perhaps the day's most important play, sprinting across the field to bring down Johnson, preventing a defensive touchdown that could have swung the game.

"In a weird way, I look forward to those moments to put some contact out there and make a tackle," McCarthy said. "At the end of the day, you have to do your job as a quarterback. When everything goes wrong, try to find a way to make it right."

Asian technology stocks jumped on Tuesday as investors piled back into the sector, following the reinstatement of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang by its board.

Key Nvidia partners and suppliers in Asia saw their shares skyrocket. Taiwan's TSMC, a critical partner to the U.S. chip giant, surged 9.85%, with Hon Hai Precision Industry — known also as Foxconn — also up by 9.95%. Nvidia partner Mediatek also spiked 9.91%. Taipei's stock exchange has a daily price limit of 10% for stocks.

In South Korea, Nvidia suppliers Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix jumped 6.7% each.The two companies, which together make up over 40% of the Kospi's weighting, sent the index up over 6%. South Korea's LG Electronics soared 15.86%.

The rise in the index triggered the Korea Exchange's sidecar on the index early in the day, reportedly making it the 100th sidecar the KRX has activated. Japan's Softbank Group Corp, a major investor in the chip space, climbed over 8% before paring gains to 4.66%.

Semiconductor test company Advantest, as well as counterpart Tokyo Electron, jumped 12% and 7%, respectively. Both companies are collaborators with Nvidia.

The exuberance did not follow into European markets at the open, with the region's technology sector down 0.3% in early deals, weighing on a broader 0.14% rise in the benchmark Stoxx 600 index. Europe's tech index popped more than 5% on Monday.

"Jensen's return is particularly welcome for Asian technology stocks, many of which suffered collateral damage from Nvidia's leadership crisis," Charu Chanana, Chief Investment Strategist at Saxo said in a note Tuesday morning.

The recovery will likely favor established companies with visible orders, strong balance sheets and pricing power, compared to those who rely on "years of uninterrupted AI spending," Chanana added.

Nvidia's stock had closed up 31% following Huang's reinstatement. It had previously shed nearly 33% from the time Huang admitted to an extramarital affair on Nov. 8.

#2733. Skip Bayless takes subtle shot at Micah Parsons with a Patrick Mahomes reality check

Micah Parsons is expected to make his long-awaited return from injury this week but Skip Bayless seems to have questioned why it took him so long.

Parsons injured his left knee in Week 15 of the 2025 season and it is understood he could now make his comeback on Wednesday when the Green Bay Packers clash with the Los Angeles Rams.

After that news emerged, Bayless took to X to make a comparison between the Packers star and Patrick Mahomes, who also injured his knee around the same time last year but returned to the field at the beginning of the 2026 season.

He wrote: "Patrick Mahomes and Micah Parsons both injured their knees last December 14 and needed surgery. Mahomes returned for this season's opener September 14 and has played all 10 games. Parsons is 'expected to play' for the first time this Wednesday night November 25."

Although it was not mentioned in Bayless' tweet, Parsons also tore his meniscus. That injury, combined with his ACL tear, typically takes anywhere between 8-12 months to recover from, so Parsons is still technically on track to recover as expected.

That is just one argument fans have made when hitting back at Bayless' comments on the Packers' ace, with one writing: "Parsons and Mahomes didn't have the same injury. While both tore their ACL, Micah also tore his Meniscus. So he had surgery for the ACL and then the other one in June. Reality is – his knee was more messed up than Mahomes' was."

A second commented: "How ignorant. Parsons had an additional meniscus tear."

Several were also quick to point out that Parsons had his surgery later than Mahomes and unlike the Kansas City Chiefs star, he had to have another one in June: "Parsons also had his surgery 2 weeks later than Mahomes and had another one in June."

The size of the defensive end compared to quarterback Mahomes and the fact that they play different positions was also brought up in response to Bayless, with a fan saying: "Parsons had a more significant tear, is 20 pounds heavier, and as a recall, had a setback in the summertime. Two different issues."

"Parsons is a DE he needs to make sure that knee is strong enough to get off. QB is a little easier coming back from that," added another.

A study also found that, on average, NFL players need 11.5 months to recover from ACL surgery, which is longer than soccer stars due to the high-impact nature of their sport. In turn, with defensive end likely a more demanding position than quarterback on the physical front and additional surgery required, Parsons' slower return time probably isn't that surprising in reality.

#2732. David Bailey already drawing Hall of Fame comparison 10 games into Jets career

Demario Davis is in his 15th NFL season, and he has played with some greats.

The Jets linebacker sees traits in rookie edge rusher David Bailey that he has observed in some of those great ones. Davis praised Bailey's "process" on Monday, saying the rookie does not let outside factors affect his preparation routine, similarly to the best players he has been around.

"I don't know what all goes into his process, but I know I see him in the same spots around the building all the time," Davis said. "That type of stuff is what I've seen from guys who are now in the Hall of Fame. When you come in and you go through the week, you see them in the same spots in the building all the time, whether it was an All-Pro wide receiver or an All-Pro running back or an All-Pro quarterback, an All-Pro defensive end in Cam Jordan — a solidified Hall of Famer, one of the best I've ever seen do it — it's all about that process. Those guys have a regimen and a routine that doesn't change based on what's going on around them. That's very rare to see for a rookie."

Jets linebacker David Bailey rehabs during practice in Florham Park, N.J., Thursday, Oct. 29, 2026. Bill Kostroun for NY Post

Bailey has been a star on the field. He added two sacks against the Chargers to bring his season total to 9 ½, just shy of Hugh Douglas' Jets rookie record of 10 set in 1995. He is five away from Jevon Kearse's NFL rookie record of 14 ½, established in 1999.

Davis said Bailey has only scratched the surface of what he can do.

"He's only going to get better," the veteran linebacker said. "It's going to be scary for a lot of quarterbacks for a long time and it's going to be good for the Jets for a long time."

The Jets gave up a touchdown just before halftime Sunday when Chargers quarterback Justin Herbert ran for a 10-yard score as time expired. The Jets showed an interesting defense on the play, with three players rushing and eight players lined up on the goal line. Herbert took off, and the Jets could not bring him down. If they had tackled him, the Chargers might have been unable to even kick a field goal. Both Dane Belton and Davis missed tackles on the play.

"They had a chance to try to score on a throwing play and we had guys in the end zone," head coach Aaron Glenn said, "but then again, if he had a chance to run it, we had guys able to converge on the ball and I think if you look at that play, I think we had two missed tackles, which would've put them in a tough situation with six seconds to try to run the ball in that situation. The thing is we just got to make that tackle. We make that tackle; we put ourselves in a good position."

#2730. The Nasdaq is in freefall. Here's one stock to look at

I asked Claude after Friday's close what I should do about the tanking stock market. Its advice was surprisingly useful: avoid panic selling and look for opportunities. A 40 VIX, which it touched Friday, might provide those opportunities. Specifically, a higher VIX (higher options premiums) signals fear, but it also creates an opportunity to sell elevated premium in some premium stocks, like Amazon.

Amazon's cloud business is growing at its fastest pace in nearly five years. AWS revenue rose 39.3% YoY in the third quarter, and management reported a $578 billion backlog. Trailing 12-month operating cash flow rose 29% to $168.3 billion. However, infrastructure investment accelerated, resulting in more than $42 billion in negative cash flow. The competing forces of accelerating growth and the cost of supplying it could keep the stock in its range over the next four weeks. Its shares are only off 14% from its recent highs, comparatively better than the Nasdaq Composite's near 19% decline.

AWS demand supports the lower end of the range. The backlog gives Amazon a basis for adding capacity even as investors scrutinize the cash cost. Cash spending could temper an upside rerating.

The next leg higher may require evidence that new capacity can earn attractive returns at this scale, and that evidence may be in short supply over the next couple of months.

Sell the Dec. 18, 2026 AMZN 235/285 strangle for an indicative $5.90 credit per share.

Max reward — $590 per strangle if AMZN finishes between $235 and $285 at expiration, before fees.

Breakevens at expiration — $229.10 and $290.90.

Max risk — Unlimited on the uncovered call, but one should think of the trade as risking getting short at a net price above $290, a nearly 13% premium to today's closing price. The downside loss would reach $22,910 per strangle if AMZN fell to zero. The credit is roughly 2.3% of a $260 reference share price over four weeks, or nearly 30% on a simple annualized basis. That comparison uses the share price as its denominator. Actual return on capital depends on margin and the cost to close or settle the trade.

Buying in bear markets, as the Nasdaq just touched, is never easy. But those opportunities don't present themselves too often. What are you going to do with it?

#2728. Spurs roll past Kings despite Victor Wembanyama missing first game of the season, De'Aaron Fox sidelined

The San Antonio Spurs were without both Victor Wembanyama and De'Aaron Fox on Monday night.

That didn't stop them from rolling past the Sacramento Kings.

Wembanyama was ruled out of the Spurs' 123-107 win over the Kings due to back tightness, the team announced shortly before the game was set to tip off at the Golden 1 Center. Fox is dealing with a shin contusion, and was ruled out at the same time.

While losing two star players for any contest is undoubtedly concerning, the two injuries were described as simply "bumps and bruises" by San Antonio Express-News' Jeff McDonald. Spurs coach Mitch Johnson said both players are not expected to be sidelined long, but would not yet declare they would be ready to return for their NBA Cup matchup against the Portland Trail Blazers on Wednesday.

The game against the Kings is the first that Wembanyama has missed all season. He's averaged 29.9 points, 12.3 rebounds and 3.9 blocks through the first 17 games of the season, all of which are career highs for the 22-year-old. It was his second 50-point game already this season, following a 52-point performance against the Dallas Mavericks earlier this fall.

Fox has averaged 12.4 points and 5.8 assists per game this season. The 28-year-old is in his second full season with the Spurs, who landed him in a deal with the Kings during the 2024-25 campaign. Johnson said Fox was injured when he was intentionally tripped by a Pelicans player.

And though the Kings jumped up early in the opening frame, the Spurs rallied and led the entire second half to secure the 16-point win. Stephon Castle led the way with a near-triple-double. He had 28 points, 11 assists and eight rebounds on the night. Julian Champagnie added 22 points, and Luke Kornet finished with 18 points and 11 rebounds.

Domantas Sabonis led the Kings with 20 points, 12 rebounds and eight assists in the loss, which dropped them to 2-15 on the season. Zach Lavine added 18 points, and De'Andre Hunter finished with 15 points and eight assists.

The Spurs now hold a 15-3 record, which is tied for the best in the league with the Los Angeles Lakers. Though both Wembanyama and Fox should be back on the floor very soon, the team is clearly capable of holding things down without them when necessary.

#2727. Can returning Beattie and Haughey make an impact?

Michael McArdle has decided the upcoming friendly double-header against Greece is the perfect time to bring back Kerry Beattie and Ella Haughey from the international wilderness.

Glentoran midfielder Chloe McCarron is also in under McArdle for the first time after a lengthily absence, but the Glentoran midfielder would likely have been included sooner if not for a spell on the sidelines through injury.

Beattie won her last cap a year ago while Haughey will hope to make a senior debut in one of the two games against Greece at Inver Park after she was an unused substitute in friendlies against England and Scotland in her sole call up in 2021.

The inclusion of both players, who have been in good form at their clubs, shows McArdle is willing to keep the door open for a return to the international squad if players are performing at club level.

Magill returns to NI squad for Greece friendlies

Portsmouth striker Beattie has been in and out of squads since her debut in 2021.

The 24-year-old has four goals in 13 caps, the last of which she won against Iceland in the Nations League play-off a year ago.

A prolific forward during two spells at Glentoran, she has struggled for consistency in moves across to Scotland with the likes of Glasgow City, Hearts and Aberdeen.

She left the Dons in the summer and consulted with McArdle when deciding upon her next move, before joining WSL 2 side Portsmouth.

Beattie has eight goals in all competitions for a Pompey side languishing at the bottom of the table and McArdle is pleased she has settled in well.

"Kerry had spells at Hearts and Aberdeen and for whatever reason it didn't quite work out at both, she had good spells but not a consistent run to express herself," McArdle said.

"The move to Portsmouth, I was delighted she reached out to me for some advice, it's an alignment for her and it's been a great move on and off the pitch. It's allowed her to enjoy herself but also play 90 minutes and be that focal point striker.

"She will have been disappointed not to make the squad for the [World Cup] play-off [against Portugal] with her form but she worked hard and she's back in, I'm delighted for her."

Beattie faces stiff competition for the starting striker role from the likes of returning captain Simone Magill and Kess Elmore, who impressed in the last camp and scored against Portugal, but she should receive some minutes across the two games and will be keen to grasp her chance to make an impact.

Left-back Haughey was called up to the senior squad as a 16-year-old under Kenny Shiels, but did not feature [Getty Images]

West Brom defender Haughey's last involvement with NI came when she represented the under-19s in 2023.

A Lisburn Rangers player when Kenny Shiels included her in the senior squad as a 16-year-old, Haughey then moved to Nottingham Forest before settling at the Baggies, who play in the National League.

The left-back, 22, has been a regular this season for the club in the third tier and combines playing with her university studies.

Left-back has been a problem position for NI which McArdle has been trying to address as demonstrated by him bringing in Lucy-Jayne Grant in the last camp and Haughey for this window to allow Rebecca Holloway to play as a central defender.

Haughey will be competing with Grant to fill that full-back role on the left side and McArdle believes she is more than capable of doing so having tried to bring her back into the squad earlier in his reign.

"Ella's been at West Brom for a few years now. She has real potential, strong, athletic potential. We tried to call her up sooner but there were some challenges," he explained.

"She's probably been one of their top performers and one of the top performers in the league. Delighted she's earned this opportunity and hopefully she can make her debut and achieve her first cap."

McArdle has made a concerted to continue to widen the player pool available to him as he aims to build depth ahead of the Nations League campaign next year, but if Beattie and Haughey catch the eye it will show that giving chances to those previously involved under different managers could be another possibility.

#2724. 'We are in a new chapter' - Unai Emery urges Aston Villa to embrace hostile Istanbul crowd in tonight's Galatasaray Champions League test

Emery has called on Villa to show conviction and togetherness as they prepare for a demanding Champions League outing in Turkiye. A positive result against Galatasaray would underline Villa's steady European progress and edge them closer to the last 16. However, the manager knows individual quality alone will not be enough to handle the hosts' relentless intensity.

Emery is determined that his squad will not allow the intimidating surroundings or Galatasaray's high-energy approach to disrupt their rhythm. Having already beaten Turkish opposition heavily in his previous Champions League home ties, winning 6-1 against Bursaspor with Valencia in November 2010 and 6-0 against Fenerbahce on Matchday 2, the Spaniard recognises that an away trip to Istanbul demands total discipline.

Speaking to TNT Sports, Emery said: "We have to be focused on our game plan on the field. We have to help each other as well, always being positive with each other and try to feel strong, comfortable and confident playing, even when the crowd is against us.

"On the field, it's about playing football, winning duels, trying to build and keeping our structure as strong as possible."

Beyond the atmosphere, Emery expects a stern tactical examination from a well-drilled Galatasaray side packed with quality across every department. The Villa manager singled out midfielder Lucas Torreira for particular praise, while also acknowledging the strength of Okan Buruk's wider squad as Villa continue to evolve on the continental stage.

"Especially in the press, they have very good players," Emery said. "Torreira, in the middle, is really playing fantastic. They have very good wingers. They have very good centre-backs and very good goalkeepers. I think the coach [Okan Buruk] is also doing a very good job.

Villa head into Tuesday's clash at Rams Park needing just one more goal to match their entire 2024-25 league-phase tally of 13. However, breaking down Galatasaray on home soil will be no easy task for Emery's side. The Turkish hosts have won their last three home matches against English opposition, beating Tottenham 3-2 and Liverpool 1-0 twice.

#2723. Trump administration proposes rule that could curb ESG investing in 401(k)s

The Trump administration proposed a rule on Monday that officials said they expect to severely cripple so-called ESG investing in workplace retirement plans such as 401(k)s.

The proposed regulation, if finalized, would amend provisions under the Employee Retirement Income Security Act of 1974, or ERISA, to clarify how employers and other plan "fiduciaries" can apply their duty of loyalty to 401(k) investors.

The rule affirms that the people who choose investments for their company retirement plan must make decisions "solely in the financial interests of workers and retirees, not to advance political or social agendas," U.S. Secretary of Labor Keith Sonderling said Monday in a written statement.

ESG — short for environmental, social and governance — funds let people invest according to certain values such as curbing climate change or improving corporate diversity. They're also known as socially responsible, sustainable, impact or values-based funds.

"We make clear in this rule that when you are selecting investments for a retirement plan ... you may not have any ulterior motives or collateral interests," a Labor Department official said during a press briefing Monday morning.

"We believe that will inform all fiduciaries as to whether an impact fund or ESG fund is appropriate for retirement funds," the official said. "We believe the answer will be very rarely."

Already, few 401(k) plans offer ESG funds — partly due to whipsawing federal policy around their use.

About 5% of 401(k) plans offered an ESG fund in 2025, according to a Plan Sponsor Council of America report issued earlier this month. On average, less than 1% of overall investor assets in 401(k) plans are held in ESG funds, the report said.

The Labor Department proposal, which is subject to a 60-day public comment period, comes as the Trump administration has tried to make it easier for employers to add alternative investments such as private equity funds to workplace retirement plans.

It also comes amid a broader Trump administration backlash against ESG and policies it views as "woke."

Within days of his inauguration in 2025, President Donald Trump announced the U.S. would withdraw from the Paris Agreement, blocked subsidies for electric vehicles, pushed for more fossil-fuel production, and started a "huge pushback" against diversity, equity and inclusion policies, Diana Iovanel, a senior markets economist at Capital Economics, wrote in a research note last year.

"Our country will be woke no longer," Trump said during a speech to a joint session of Congress in March 2025.

More recently, Trump and the Republican-led Congress repealed key elements of the Inflation Reduction Act, former President Joe Biden's signature law to combat climate change. In April, a White House report said ESG investing has a "significant cost to investors and the broader economy."

Monday's proposal undoes elements of a Biden administration rule in 2022, Labor Department officials said. That Biden-era regulation sought to loosen ESG investing rules put in place in 2020 by the first Trump administration. The Biden rule, for example, clarified that employers could weigh the economic effects of climate change and other ESG considerations when making investment choices, officials said at the time.

Among the ways the latest proposal seeks to curb ESG uptake in 401(k) plans is by eliminating the "tiebreaker test" and the "participant preference provision," the Labor Department said.

The tiebreaker test says, for example, that if an ESG fund is "tied" with another fund after comparing various financial factors, a 401(k) plan sponsor can choose the ESG fund because of its social benefits, according to legal experts.

Current rules also allow employers to select funds based partly on their workers' preferences, provided they still meet certain financial criteria.

Data shows that ESG funds may provide stronger financial performance than that of more traditional funds, however.

For example, sustainable funds returned investors a median 4.9% in the first half of 2026, versus 4% for traditional funds, according to a Morgan Stanley analysis in September.

"Sustainable funds also continue to outperform over the long term: A hypothetical $100 investment into a sustainable fund in December 2018 — the start date for Morningstar's sustainable fund classification — would have grown to $171 today, while investing $100 into a traditional fund over the same period of time would have become $159 today, assuming median returns were achieved in each period," according to the report.
