#2707. Sixers' record-setting wait for the West ends against Trail Blazers

Sixers' record-setting wait for the West ends against Trail Blazers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Have you noticed anything different about the Sixers' season?

No, not the fact that LeBron James and Jaylen Brown are wearing red, white and blue and have helped Philadelphia build an Eastern Conference-leading 14-3 record.

The difference is hidden in their schedule. All 17 games have come against Eastern Conference opponents.

When the Sixers welcome the Portland Trail Blazers to Xfinity Mobile Arena on Monday night, it will mark the latest they have ever gone into a season before facing a Western Conference team.

Philadelphia's 18-game wait also ties the 1979-80 Atlanta Hawks for the second latest by an Eastern Conference team. Only the 1980-81 Milwaukee Bucks went longer, meeting the West for the first time in their 21st game.

The Sixers have played who has been placed in front of them and won five consecutive games. They have already beaten the Knicks twice and own victories over Cleveland and Detroit.

Their first Western Conference matchup introduces a different comparison.

The West entered Monday with 11 teams above .500. The East had seven. Western Conference teams were also 41-20 against the East through Sunday.

Portland will not answer every question about how the Sixers compare with the other conference. It begins a more varied portion of their schedule.

The Sixers will face Memphis and Denver on consecutive nights next week. Dallas and Houston visit later in December before Philadelphia travels to play the Lakers, Clippers, Kings and Suns over a six-day stretch.

Portland is 10-6 under first-year head coach Micah Nori, the father of Phillies prospect Dante Nori.

The Blazers are averaging 116.1 points while allowing 110.3 per game, with both figures ranking in the top 10. Their 15.4 made three-pointers per game rank second in the NBA.

Deni Avdija leads Portland with 23.9 points per game. Damian Lillard is averaging 20.6 points in his return to the franchise, while 7-foot-2 center Donovan Clingan has supplied size around a deep group of guards and wings.

Clingan posted 22 points and 13 rebounds Saturday night at Madison Square Garden. Portland erased a 26-point deficit and briefly took the lead before Mikal Bridges made a game-winning three-pointer at the buzzer.

The 97-96 loss dropped the Blazers to 1-3 over their last four games. Slow starts have followed them during that stretch.

"Our last three games, we haven't gotten off to great starts, and that's just something that we got to correct," Lillard said. "Our energy and our focus and our intent has to be better from the jump. We can't let this trend continue."

The Sixers know something about overcoming early deficits. Six of their 14 victories have required double-digit comebacks.

Portland will be missing two major pieces. Ja Morant is out with a left hamstring strain, while Shaedon Sharpe remains sidelined following right meniscus surgery.

Even without Morant, the Blazers can turn to Lillard, Jrue Holiday and Scoot Henderson to initiate their offense. Avdija and Toumani Camara provide size on the perimeter, while Clingan gives Portland the kind of interior presence that has occasionally troubled the Sixers.

Anfernee Simons and Jabari Walker know the Portland organization better than any other Sixers.

The two shared the floor for 128 games with the Trail Blazers. Portland won 43 of them.

Their responsibilities have changed in Philadelphia. So have the expectations.

Simons scored 18 points in each of the Sixers' last two games and made seven three-pointers. He started in place of Tyrese Maxey against Orlando and hit the clinching three in his hometown. Three nights later, he supplied four more threes off the bench in the Sixers' 132-129 win over Milwaukee.

Walker has also found steady work during the Sixers' recent frontcourt injuries. He recorded nine points, seven rebounds and four assists against the Bucks after scoring 11 against Indiana and hitting a go-ahead three late in Orlando.

Both players recently discussed how their difficult seasons in Portland changed their perspective on filling smaller roles for a winning team.

"You feel appreciative of the times where you go into a situation where your role might be a little bit less, but you're winning," Simons said. "That's something that you can't take for granted, for sure, because it's hard to win in the NBA. We've seen it firsthand."

Their former team will now see them operating within a deeper rotation than the one Philadelphia carried through much of the past week.

Brown, Dean Wade and Adem Bona were all listed as probable on the Sixers' initial injury report after missing time.

Brown and Wade have each missed the last two games. Bona has been sidelined since Nov. 4 because of a cervical spine sprain. Dominick Barlow returned Saturday and scored 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting in his season debut.

Joel Embiid was added to the injury report Monday morning as questionable because of an illness. His availability will determine how the Sixers approach Clingan and Robert Williams III inside.

The Sixers have leaned on smaller lineups and reserve contributors while Nurse has managed injuries across the roster. Monday could give him more options, even if Embiid cannot play.

FanDuel Sportsbook listed the Sixers as 3.5-point favorites Monday afternoon. Philadelphia was -152 on the moneyline, Portland was +128 and the over-under was 229.5.

The Blazers are 7-4 on the road. The Sixers are 7-2 at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

After 17 games within their own conference, the Sixers will finally see what their early success looks like against the other side.

#2705. Observations after Sixers survive Bucks' late surge, reach 14-3

Observations after Sixers survive Bucks' late surge, reach 14-3 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers survived the Bucks' late surge and reached 14-3 on the season Saturday afternoon with a 132-129 win at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Tyler Herro nearly sent the game to overtime with a buzzer-beating three-pointer, but replays showed that he released his shot late.

Herro had a massive performance for the 1-16 Bucks, posting a career-high 50 points on 15-for-23 shooting, 11 rebounds and seven assists.

Joel Embiid had 26 points, a season-high 17 rebounds and nine assists for the Sixers.

LeBron James (21 points), Tyrese Maxey (17 points) and Anfernee Simons (18) were among the seven Sixers to score in double figures.

The Sixers were down Jaylen Brown (left hamstring strain), Dean Wade (left hamstring tightness/soreness), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear).

Next up for the Sixers is a Monday night meeting with the Trail Blazers. Here are observations on their victory over the Bucks:

Maxey returned to the Sixers' starting five after missing the team's comeback win Wednesday over the Magic with right foot soreness. He was joined by VJ Edgecombe, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, James and Embiid.

James threw down a dunk on the day's first play. Embiid then dished to a cutting Edgecombe for a layup that put the Sixers up 4-0.

The Bucks quickly found a foothold in the game.

Embiid missed his first four shots, including two that were swatted by Myles Turner. Maxey began 1 for 5 from the floor. Herro canned a three-pointer to give Milwaukee a 15-8 lead.

The Sixers entered the game with a minus-13.2 net rating in first quarters, which ranked 28th in the NBA. They faced a 30-29 deficit after Saturday's first period.

Even with Maxey available again, Labaron Philon Jr. deservingly stayed in the Sixers' rotation following an excellent outing in Orlando.

He scored two points in six first-half minutes. The 20-year-old made a rookie mistake at the tail end of the first quarter, fouling Herro on a three-point attempt.

Dominick Barlow checked in for his first action of the season with 2:51 remaining in the first quarter. He'd been sidelined for the Sixers' first 16 games because of a lower back stress reaction.

Barlow scored his first points early in the second quarter when he tipped in a Maxey miss. He then teamed up with James in crowd-pleasing style, sprinting ahead of the pack on a fast break and flushing home the 22-time All-Star's long-distance lob.

The afternoon was not devoid of rust for Barlow. Shortly after that alley-oop, he grabbed an offensive rebound, dribbled out to the perimeter and fired an open three-pointer … which he air balled short.

To Barlow's credit, he took another three early in the fourth quarter and drained it. He logged 11 minutes and was quite productive, tallying 12 points on 5-for-6 shooting and four rebounds.

"It felt great," Barlow said of his three. "Got to shoot the ball with confidence. … I've obviously got to get a game feel; I've been missing that. But I worked too hard (in the offseason) to not shoot the ball when I'm open."

Kyle Kuzma hit a three late in the second quarter to build the Bucks' lead to 59-52. After Sixers head coach Nick Nurse's timeout, the team closed the first half on a 8-0 run featuring an Embiid dunk.

The Sixers went just 3 for 16 from long range in the first half, but their low-turnover ways helped mitigate those shooting struggles.

They committed a single turnover in the first half and finished with four giveaways. That's tied for their lowest ever on record in a single game. The Sixers have the NBA's lowest turnover percentage outside of garbage time, according to Cleaning the Glass.

"No matter who you're playing against, teams try to turn you over, they try to speed you up," James said. "Us taking care of the ball, that's a lot of the reason why we won this game."

The Sixers' shooters were bound to start sinking some open looks eventually. Simons, Caldwell-Pope and Edgecombe all made threes late in the third quarter and the team's lead grew as high as 13 points.

Thanks largely to Herro's individuals exploits, the Bucks stayed competitive. AJ Green buried a corner three to cut Milwaukee's deficit to 106-103 with 8:10 to go in the game.

James sought out his shot much more following the Sixers' timeout and did so with great success. He scored four buckets inside over the next four minutes, including a big slam that put the Sixers up 121-112.

It appeared the main questions left were whether Embiid could reach a triple-double and just how many points Herro would rack up.

However, the Bucks forced the Sixers to work down the stretch. Herro got to the 50-point milestone and trimmed the Sixers' lead to two points when he converted a layup with 19.2 seconds left.

The Sixers safely navigated to the final buzzer by the slimmest of margins. Embiid made four late free throws and everyone on the Sixers' sideline was relieved to see that Herro hadn't quite released his jumper in time.

"I try not to complain too much about any win in this league," Nurse said. "The message is we've got to finish a little better."

#2703. LeBron Log No. 6: A Rocky night, a shooting lesson and a fight over ‘Bron'

LeBron Log No. 6: A Rocky night, a shooting lesson and a fight over 'Bron' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

It was another eventful week for LeBron James.

That's why we started the LeBron Log in the first place.

The King spent his latest week taking his teammates to the movies, fighting over his name in federal court and producing his best three-point shooting performance as a Sixer days after recording a podcast about the craft.

This week also marks 20 years since James faced the Sixers alongside Allen Iverson.

James found an appropriate choice for the Sixers' latest team outing.

He organized a private screening of I Play Rocky on Friday night for players, their families and members of the basketball operations staff. The film follows Sylvester Stallone's fight to get the original Rocky made while insisting that he play the title character.

"I mean, it's Rocky. We in Philly," James said. "I heard it was a great movie, and we had a night off. I've got a few connections, and I was able to bring that to my teammates and this organization for us to watch it. I thought it was a great movie. Unbelievably cast. Everyone looked like exactly who they were portraying. That is Hollywood for you. It was pretty cool."

The Sixers shared photos of James alongside Jaylen Brown, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Labaron Philon Jr., Jabari Walker, Tacko Fall and Adem Bona.

"Movie night, courtesy of LeBron," the team wrote.

The nationwide release of I Play Rocky came less than two weeks before Philadelphia celebrates the 50th anniversary of the original film on Dec. 3.

Nick Nurse could not attend because of a previous commitment, but he received positive reviews from his players.

"They went, they said it was very good, they loved it," Nurse said. "The big takeaway was how much Anthony actually looked like Rocky. Or Sylvester Stallone. One in the same."

James' first season in Philadelphia now includes a private Rocky screening. A trip up the Art Museum steps remains available for a future edition.

James and Steve Nash devoted the latest episode of their Mind the Game podcast to the art of shooting.

The two examined shooting form, confidence, repetitions, free throws and some of their favorite jumpers. James also revealed one detail that can affect what he does after catching the ball.

"I'm a guy who don't like shooting against the laces," James said. "So sometimes I'll come up on a quick stop and I'm about to raise and I can feel the laces not fixed. I'll turn that into a pump fake."

The episode was released Nov. 17, while James was experiencing an extended struggle from three-point range. It did not provide an instant fix.

James went 1 for 5 from three against Orlando and missed his lone attempt against Milwaukee. He entered Monday night's meeting with Portland shooting 21.7 percent from deep through his first 13 games.

James made all three of his first-half attempts from three. He ended the second quarter by pump-faking, stepping to his left and beating the buzzer with a corner three that tied the game at 69.

Nobody could see where the laces sat on the catch. The pump fake was clear.

James finished 4 for 5 from three and scored a season-high 23 points on 8-for-13 shooting. It was his third consecutive game with at least 20 points, following 22 against Orlando and 21 against Milwaukee.

The Sixers lost to Portland, 128-111, but James produced his best long-range performance since joining them.

Six days after discussing the art of shooting, he provided a brief demonstration.

James is also attempting to protect one of his most recognizable nicknames.

James and LBJ Trademarks LLC filed a federal lawsuit on Nov. 17 against PlayVision Inc. and its founders over a sports-betting research app initially called "Bron."

The complaint alleges that the company used an AI-generated version of James in an advertisement without his permission. The figure wore a No. 23 jersey with "Bron" across the back and resembled James through its voice, build and tattoos.

It also scrolled through wagers involving NBA players.

The lawsuit alleges that the advertisement falsely created the impression that James endorsed the app and suggested that he considered betting on NBA games. The complaint said the video received roughly 1 million views before it was removed.

"The integrity of NBA competition, and the public's confidence that players are not betting on the games they play, is of paramount importance to the league, its players and its fans," the complaint said. "Any suggestion that an active NBA player is betting on NBA games is therefore far from a trivial matter, and it should not be used for clicks and views."

The app offered odds comparisons, betting trends and tracking tools but did not directly accept wagers, according to its developer.

PlayVision renamed it "Evie" on Nov. 18, one day after James filed the lawsuit. James is seeking an injunction and unspecified damages. The case remains active in the Southern District of New York.

CLEVELAND – NOVEMBER 25: LeBron James #23 of the Cleveland Cavaliers looks to pass off when double teamed by the Philadelphia 76ers November 25, 2006 at The Quicken Loans Arena in Cleveland, Ohio. Copyright 2006 NBAE (Photo by David Liam Kyle/NBAE via Getty Images)

James spent Thanksgiving weekend in 2006 on the other side of the Sixers-Cavaliers matchup.

On Nov. 25, 2006, a 21-year-old James recorded 25 points, 11 assists, four rebounds and one block in Cleveland's 108-95 win over Philadelphia.

Donning the Cavs' old-school orange threads, he played nearly 41 minutes.

Iverson led the Sixers with 31 points. Andre Iguodala added 17 points and eight rebounds, while Kyle Korver made three three-pointers off the bench.

James recorded 11 assists that night. Nearly 20 years later, another 11-assist performance against Indiana moved him past Jason Kidd for third place in NBA history.

The 2006 meeting also came near the end of Iverson's original run with Philadelphia. The Sixers traded him to Denver less than a month later.

James was 21. Iverson was nearing his departure.

Twenty years later, James is wearing a Sixers uniform and Iverson's No. 3 hangs above his home floor.

#2702. Tennis ball protests could return to the Bundesliga as ultras ramp up pressure on DFB

Less than three years after German football fans successfully fought off the DFL's plans to bring in an external investor, tennis balls could once again be flying onto Bundesliga pitches.

According to a report from BILD, ultra groups across Germany are mobilising ahead of a decisive DFB vote concerning the future of the Regionalliga on 18 December, with almost 100 active fan groups mentioned in the report.

Banners are expected to appear in stadia across the pyramid this weekend, with further demonstrations potentially leading to match interruptions in the weeks that follow.

At the centre of the dispute is the fourth tier of German football, where the current structure of the five regional divisions means that not every champion is guaranteed promotion to the 3. Liga.

Supporters are backing the so-called 'compass model', which would see the Regionalliga reduced to four divisions, with all four champions earning automatic promotion from the 2028/29 campaign.

The proposal, which is also backed by DFB president Bernd Neuendorf, will be put to a vote at next month's extraordinary congress.

An alternative proposal to expand the 3. Liga to 22 teams has met considerable opposition from organised support groups due to the prospect of additional midweek fixtures and the subsequent travelling difficulties for fans.

The coming weeks could therefore see a return of the scenes witnessed across German football in early 2024, when persistent fan protests ultimately contributed to the collapse of the DFL's proposed investor deal

#2701. Trump sealed deal for Nvidia to rehire Jensen Huang as CEO

The stunning decision by Nvidia's board to bring back Jensen Huang as CEO was sealed by a personal promise from President Donald Trump on Sunday that Huang had not broken national security laws, three people familiar with the board's thinking told CNBC.

Several other factors also set the stage for Huang's return and accelerated discussions with him over the weekend, said the people, who spoke on condition of anonymity to discuss internal deliberations close to Nvidia's board.

A sharp stock price drop after his resignation and questions about the leadership of interim CEO Colette Kress also spurred the board's decision, the people said.

"I convinced them," Trump told CNBC's Eamon Javers in a phone interview Monday morning, shortly after Nvidia announced Huang's return.

"I made the deal," the president said. "It was a lot easier to convince them yesterday than it was a week and a half ago.

Trump said Nvidia's board asked for guarantees that Huang, who resigned less than two weeks ago, was not a threat to national security.

"And we gave them assurances, at least with everything we know," the president said. "We believe he's fine. He's absolutely fine."

Senior Trump administration officials reached out to the board overnight Friday to inform them that Huang had not violated U.S. national security laws, people familiar with the board's actions said.

After the White House outreach, the board contacted Huang, who joined meetings on Saturday and Sunday to discuss his potential return, the people said.

Huang's spokesman did not respond to a request for comment when asked about his contact with the board and other events leading up to his return.

The Nvidia founder resigned on Nov. 12 after reports that he had an affair with an Nvidia employee and with Alice Huang, a former executive at the Singapore-based company Megaspeed who is suspected of being a spy for China. A report of an affair with a second Nvidia employee broke right after he quit.

Nvidia's board named Kress, the company's chief financial officer, as the interim CEO while it searched for a permanent successor.

But within days of Kress taking over, some employees and investors who spoke to CNBC questioned her stewardship, as many of them clamored for Huang's return.

Kress' performances at an all-hands company meeting on Wednesday and on Nvidia's earnings call with analysts a day earlier were seen by some investors as uninspiring and underwhelming.

As the board faced those concerns amid a stock drop of more than 25% over the past week, Huang was aggressively working the phones with major tech CEOs who wanted to see him return to Nvidia, according to sources close to Huang.

In the days after he resigned, Huang spoke with OpenAI CEO Sam Altman, OpenAI President Greg Brockman, and Elon Musk, CEO of SpaceX and Tesla, one of the sources said.

Huang wanted his job back, but he was not sure what the return path looked like, the sources said.

On Nov. 17, less than a week after quitting, Huang issued a public statement saying he was open to returning as CEO.

"I've seen an outpouring of calls from NVIDIA's employees, investors, business partners, and customers for me to return as CEO," he said.

"NVIDIA is a once-in-a-generation company," he added. "If the Board of Directors decides that my return is in the best interest of our shareholders, our employees, the company, and America, I would be honored to answer these calls and return as CEO."

On the same day, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, Harvey Jones, told analysts on the company's earnings call that the board did "not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO."

Kress, the following day, told company employees at the all-hands meeting, "Let's move forward."

Nvidia's public stance against Huang returning came as members of Congress moved ahead with plans to interview him at hearings and as criticism grew over the company's business in Asia.

The Department of Justice and the Commerce Department, in an unusual joint public statement on Nov. 17, said they were investigating Megaspeed for possible diversion of advanced Nvidia semiconductor chips to China in violation of U.S. export control law.

"Sensitive technologies like semiconductors are among the most valuable and strategic assets of the U.S. economy," the departments said.

But days later, senior Trump administration officials contacted Nvidia's board to tell them that Jensen Huang himself was not a risk, according to the sources.

The Department of Commerce added Megaspeed to a trade blacklist later Monday.

Trump, during his interview Monday with CNBC, was asked what Attorney General Todd Blanche had told him about potential criminal liability for Huang and the question of whether Nvidia chips had ended up in China in violation of U.S. export laws.

"They did a pretty good investigation, and they felt that he, himself, was exonerated," Trump said.

Both Trump and Vice President JD Vance became convinced that Huang was a singular figure who is preventing closed-source model AI companies, such as Anthropic, from getting too powerful, according to a person familiar with the matter, who spoke on condition of anonymity to disclose private discussions.

The person said Trump and Vance were briefed on the national security elements of the situation and saw no evidence that Huang did anything illegal or untoward in that regard.

Trump believes his administration's close working relationship with Huang gives the U.S. its best opportunity to protect national security and prevent cutting-edge AI chips from reaching banned customers, according to the person.

Trump said he spoke with Huang on Sunday, after Nvidia had voted unanimously to reinstate him.

"I called him up, and I said: 'I ask you to do one thing, Jensen. Work hard,'" Trump said.

"He knows what to do and what not to do," Trump said. "But I just said work hard because if you work hard, there's nobody that can capture what you've done. Nobody, nobody close."

— CNBC's Kevin Breuninger and Kif Leswing contributed to this article.

#2700. Roob's Observations as Eagles' defensive statement sparks win over Steelers

Roob's Observations as Eagles' defensive statement sparks win over Steelers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

For the first time in forever, the Eagles won a football game Sunday.

The Eagles took down the 6-3 Steelers, 19-16, at the Linc, ending their nightmarish seven-game losing streak and extending their 60-year home winning streak vs. the Steelers to 12 straight since 1965.

They didn't make it easy on themselves, failing to score a 2nd-half touchdown for the sixth time this year and allowing Aaron Rodgers to make it a three-point game with a 20-yard touchdown pass to Max Hurleman in his first career game with 4:20 left. But Joe Milton, in his second NFL start, threw a seven-yard 1st-down pass to DeVonta Smith on a 3rd-and-6 to clinch the win.

Here's our 10 Instant Observations on the Eagles' first win since Lane Johnson was playing right tackle.

1. When Jeff Lurie is ultimately evaluating Nick Sirianni after this miserable season, the first question he'll ask himself is whether Sirianni's voice was still getting through to the players. Whether they were still putting forth effort and playing hard for their beleaguered coach. If the answer to that question is ever no, you move on. If it's yes, it makes you think twice about making a change because if guys are still playing hard after losing seven straight games, that means the coach still has buy-in from the locker room and his message is still getting through. This was the kind of game that tells you that, yeah, this team is still playing tough, hard-nosed football and nobody can watch this one and question their effort. Does it mean Sirianni will be back? Not necessarily. But it sure doesn't hurt when a 2-7 team plays like this. That speaks volumes. Still seven games to go and we'll see what direction it goes, but this performance was an encouraging one when it comes to asking whether Sirianni is still connecting with his players.

2. Gotta love what we saw out of the Eagles' defense in its first game without Jordan Davis. They played hard, they played physical, they played tough and they played smart. They were excellent against the run, they got decent pressure on Aaron Rodgers and they made big plays in the red zone, limiting Pittsburgh to field goals on drives to the 7, 22 and 19 before the late touchdown. I loved Jeremiah Trotter's stop on Jaylen Warren on a 4th-and-1 at the Steelers' 40-yard-line on Pittsburgh's first drive, and I loved Jonathan Jones' diving pass breakup at the goal-line late in the second quarter after he had just replaced Riq Woolen a few snaps earlier. That was a huge goal-line stand after the Steelers dinked and dunked down to a 1st-and-goal on the 8-yard-line with two minutes left, forcing them to settle for a field goal after a 17-play drive. And then a huge stop to open the third quarter after Jaydon Blue fumbled the opening kickoff at the Eagles' own 24-yard-line, again forcing a Pittsburgh field goal. This was the 25th-ranked defense in the NFL when the day began, but they played like a top-10 unit, holding the Steelers to 255 yards, 5-for-17 on third down, just 3.6 yards per rushing attempt and limiting Rodgers to just 5.2 yards per attempt. And Quinyon Mitchell held D.K. Metcalf to one catch for two yards before he was ejected for being a lunatic. How do you replace a Jordan Davis? Not with one guy but with everybody else picking up their game a little bit, and that's exactly what we saw. Terrific effort by everybody on defense.

3. On top of six sacks against the Giants, the Eagles had five Sunday against the Steelers, giving them more in the last two games (11) than in their first eight games (10). Byron Young, getting increased reps in Jordan Davis's absence, had two and Jonathan Greenard, Nolan Smith and practice squad call-up Gabe Hall had one apiece. Really encouraging to see the pressure from up front, and that's 6.0 sacks total and four straight games with a sack for Smith, longest streak by an Eagle since Haason Reddick had a sack in five straight games in 2022. Granted, Aaron Rodgers is a statue back there and you're not going to face many 42-year-old quarterbacks. But it's also two games in a row where the Eagles have generated good pressure, and that's definitely a good sign. The Eagles have too much talent up front to be a team that gets one or two sacks a game. Too many high draft picks and big contracts. The last two weeks is the blueprint for what this front should look like. Two straight games the Eagles have held an opponent under 20 points, and that's not happening if your guys up front aren't dominating. Sunday they were.

4. This offense is so bad in the second half it's hard to comprehend. They did manage a couple field goals Sunday – one in the third quarter, one in the fourth – but they have still scored an astonishing 46 points after halftime through 10 games. That's their fewest since 1972, when they had 26 through 10 games and second -fewest since 1942. That's all coaching. Not that they're setting any scoring records in the first half, but give defensive coaches a chance to make adjustments at halftime and the Eagles don't have a prayer in the second half. They have four 2nd-half touchdowns, only two of them since Jalen Hurts was lost for the season. They won a game Sunday because the defense was really good, but it's just hard to fathom an offense that is averaging 4 ½ points per game in the second half. That's almost impossible to do.

5. Usually, if I'm writing about Jake Elliott after a game it's because of a big miss or a slump. Not this time. Elliott was huge Sunday in a game where the Eagles really needed him to be huge. He made field goals of 29, 41, 53 and 55 yards, the first time in franchise history an Eagles kicker has made two field goals of at least 53 yards in the same game. The 55-yarder was so clutch, extending the Eagles' lead to 10 points and two possessions with 4:20 left. Elliott was 3-9 in 2024 and 2025 combined from 53 yards and out, and you couldn't help but wonder if the 2017 Super Bowl star still had it. But the Eagles believed he could still make big kicks, and he's been proving them right all year. Elliott is 4-for-4 this year from 53 yards and out and the 55-yarder was his third this year from 55 and out – more than he had in 2024 and 2025 combined. The Eagles have gotten a lot of things wrong over the past year, but they sure got this one right.

6. Joe Milton still has a ways to go to become a consistent NFL starting quarterback. He's missing open guys, he's firing the ball too hard at times for his receivers to catch and his decision making has been uneven. But he showed some serious clutch genes with that 3rd-and-6 completion to DeVonta Smith to turn a precarious three-point lead into a win. Milton only completed 11 passes Sunday and didn't really do any damage running, and he just needs time in this offense, time with these linemen, time with these receivers. I'm still not sure he gives the Eagles a better chance at winning than Tanner McKee, but his skill set is intriguing. He's got that huge arm and we saw last week he can run. He's kind of like a young pitcher that comes up with this 101-mph fastball but really needs to learn to throw a change-up before he can really be effective. Milton has some ability, he's just got to refine it. But that pass to DeVonta speaks volumes about his confidence and his ability to perform at a high level under extreme pressure. That can't be taught.

7. First three possessions: Field goal, field goal, touchdown, 123 yards, five first downs. OK, not bad. Next eight drives? Punt, end of half, punt, field goal, punt, punt, field goal, ballgame, 91 yards, six first downs. This is my biggest issue with Sean Mannion. He may have some good ideas that generate points in the first or second quarter, but once the other team figures things out he doesn't have an answer. The offense looked so good early Sunday and then just deflated. They managed just 214 total yards, 11 first downs, 3.5 yards per play and 3.5 yards per rushing attempt. Just putrid production and we just keep seeing the same pattern. I just don't think Mannion has the play-calling chops to counter when defenses adjust to what he's doing. Sure seems like he doesn't.

8. Saquon Barkley once again has a big first half followed by a nothing second half. This has been a pattern all year and again I point the finger at Mannion because Barkley is the same running back before halftime as after halftime, but the holes aren't there, the plays aren't there, the opportunities aren't there. Barkley was 9-for-59 before halftime Sunday and 8-for-16 after halftime before shutting down a little early with an injury. First half this year, Barkley is 56-for-291 (5.2 average) with 11 first downs and four touchdowns. Second half 30-for-80 (2.7) with two first downs. He's got eight runs this year of 10 yards or more, seven in the first half and one in the second half. He's got runs of 16, 17, 29, 38 and 42 yards before halftime, but his longest run after halftime is 12 yards. Barkley isn't transforming into Darnell Autry at halftime. Same elite back. This is solely on the play caller.

9. Let's talk about Nolan Smith and Jalyx Hunt. Smith picked up his sixth sack of the year and Hunt still has none, although he did have one in the second quarter negated by a ridiculous illegal contact penalty on Quinyon Mitchell. But in the big picture, if you can only keep one of these guys, I think the narrative has changed since opening day. Coming off last year, everybody agreed that Hunt was the better of the two young ascending edge rushers, and if Howie could only keep one it would have to be Hunt. But Smith has played that narrative into the ground. He's been very good and very consistent while Hunt just hasn't gotten the production you'd expect coming off such a dominating training camp. Now, with Jordan Davis gone maybe Roseman can keep both of them. But I think it's more likely Smith gets the contract extension and Hunt is either traded this offseason or plays out the final year of his deal in 2027 and the Eagles draft an edge in a 2027 draft loaded with them. Either way, great to see Smith giving the Eagles the kind of pass rush you expect from a 1st-round pick.

10. One thing the Eagles are apparently good at is winning football games without forcing any turnovers. They have zero takeaways in any of their three wins this year and this is only the fourth time in franchise history they've won three games in a season without a takeaway. And the first time ever they've recorded three wins in a row in games that they didn't have any takeaways. The lack of takeaways is truly astounding. The Eagles have played 10 games and they have zero takeaways in seven of them, the most takeaway-free games they've ever had through 10 games and actually their 2nd-most in any entire season – they had nine games without a takeaway in 2012. Their three interceptions match the fewest in franchise history after 10 games – they also had three in 1998 and 2020. Their five takeaways so far actually ties the fewest in any 10-game span in Eagles history – they went 10 games with five takeaways in 2012. And their three INTs also ties the fewest in Eagles history after 10 games, achieved also by the 1998 and 2020 teams. Andrew Mukuba could have had one down at the goal-line Sunday, but really with the talent on this defense these takeaway numbers are just hard to believe. When they win, they're winning despite a complete absence of takeaways. Tough way to play football.

11. Watching Jeremiah Trotter Jr. in his sixth NFL start I can't help thinking there's no way you can take this dude off the field. He's too good. Thirteen tackles against the Steelers and some big hits and big plays. I don't care where he was drafted or what his contract is or how big he is or what his 40 time is, he can't come off the field. With Zack Baun out for at least the next month, we'll get a good long look at both Trotter and Jihaad Campbell, last year's 1st-round pick. And Campbell hasn't been terrible and had his best game this year Sunday, giving us a taste of what's possible with him. If you want the best combo, you've got to forget rotating Campbell and Trotter and play Baun and Trotter. But can you sit a 1st-round pick who hasn't been awful? It makes you wonder about Baun's future here. He's been fantastic, a 1st-team all-pro in 2024 and a Pro Bowler in 2024 and 2025. Huge part of a championship team. But he also turn 30 next month and Howie Roseman might decide that as the Eagles rebuild from this terrible season they have to move forward with Trotter, who's 23, and Campbell, who's 22. Hard to believe the team that brought you Moses Fokou, Jamar Chaney, Matt McCoy, Quiton Caver and Omar Gaither has too much talent at linebacker.

#2699. James sees a lot to like — and improve — for East-leading Sixers

James sees a lot to like — and improve — for East-leading Sixers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James likes to refer to the NBA season as a "marathon."

A few miles in, the Sixers lead the Eastern Conference pack.

With 17 regular-season contests down of 82, the team sits at 14-3, which is 2.5 games ahead of the second-seeded Celtics. The Sixers squeaked out their latest win, a 132-129 decision Saturday over the cellar-dwelling Bucks, but the bottom line is they've become accustomed to finding many routes to victory.

"We want to build habits," James said. "We want to try to take steps forward every game that we get the opportunity. I know sometimes it doesn't happen that way, but we want to do it.

"We want to strive to be as great as we can offensively, defensively and on special teams. A win is a win, but there's ways to win and ways to try to continue to build habits over the course of a marathon."

All sorts of Sixers games have gone down to the wire and they've won every single one.

The team is an incredible 12-0 in clutch situations, defined as games with a point differential of five or under in the final five minutes. The Sixers rank third in the NBA in clutch offensive rating (140.3) and third in clutch net rating (plus-42.0).

"It's good to build that part of our game," James said. "Obviously, we wanted to close the game out a lot better. But a team like that, we know they're going to be scrappy. They're going to play as hard as they can for 48 minutes. … We can learn from that."

James was the centerpiece of the Sixers' fourth-quarter offense Saturday. He scored 13 of his 21 points between the 7:46 and 3:13 mark of the fourth.

The four-time MVP got two buckets on no-nonsense drives and another on an after-timeout isolation hoop over rookie Brayden Burries. James also scored three times on a designed Horns action where he brought the ball up, used Tyrese Maxey's back screen at the elbow and caused serious problems for Milwaukee in the paint.

"We continue to add things to the half-court offense and also to the late-game package," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "That's something we added a few game ago. It's one of those things where, especially if we're in the bonus, we've got some big bodies that can cut down the lane and have feel for timing. It was working. Just keep going to it."

James has grown in comfort with Nurse's coaching style. He knows the Sixers expect him to be a de facto coach on the floor, too.

"I'm just trying to be there for the team in any capacity they want," he said. "Just trying to be an extension of his calls and his commands out there on the floor. Trying to make his life and his job a little bit easier as well.

"But it's been great, not only with him but the whole coaching staff. They've welcomed me with open arms and allowed me to kind of be the voice for the team every day, and take that responsibility."

After a tremendous first impression defensively and some subpar offensive nights, the Sixers' numbers have recently evened out.

The team is 12th in offensive rating outside of garbage time and 12th in defensive rating, per Cleaning the Glass. In essence, the Sixers have been a bit above average on both ends of the floor and downright brilliant in the closing minutes.

It's all added up to an early lead in a very long race.

"I think we've bought into the pass," James said. "We have so many guys that can score the ball at elite levels. … In order for us to be a super-complete team, I think the power of the pass is very infectious. And it's also key for our other guys, to make sure they feel in a rhythm and they feel confident. We've done that from Day 1 and I think it's gotten better and better. … And I think our defense has been really good for the majority of the season.

"(Today) it wasn't so great, but we've been very good defensively throughout the course of the season. … I don't want to say there's anything bad. There's so many things for us to do better for sure. There's games where we can defensive rebound a lot better and there's games where we can not turn the ball over (as much). And there's games where we can be a little more phsyical defensively at the point of attack like (today).

"I wouldn't say anything has been bad, though. That's a good thing. We want to continue to have room for improvement and we don't want to be great in November. That would be weird."

#2698. Riq Woolen ‘annoyed' by benching but happy for an Eagles win

Riq Woolen 'annoyed' by benching but happy for an Eagles win originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The veteran cornerback was pulled out of the game in the second quarter of the Eagles' 19-16 win over the Steelers in the middle of a drive. He then sat for most of the second half until injuries thrust him back into action.

"It may be frustrating at times, it may be annoying at times, but at the same time, I still got to be a great player," Woolen said.

"I'm happy we won today because that's the overall objective when we go out there is to win. So overall, I'm annoyed, but I'm still happy we won. Wins are hard in this league and we ain't won since when? I don't know, Week 3 of September? So I'm happy but annoyed. But at the end of the day, we won and that's what everybody in here wants to see."

The 27-year-old Woolen, who signed a one-year deal with the Eagles in March, has had plenty of ups and downs this season. That continued in the first half against the Steelers.

During that second quarter drive, Woolen missed two tackles and was pulled from the game in favor of veteran Jonathan Jones. Woolen said aside from those missed tackles his felt like his game was "smooth." A few plays later, Jones had a huge pass breakup on third down to force a field goal.

Woolen said he went into Sunday's game knowing there was a chance he could be taken out in favor of Jones because Woolen had previously had a "couple mental lapses," but expected it to last for just a series or two. On Sunday, it didn't look like the Eagles were planning on going back to Woolen at all.

Late in the second half, Quinyon Mitchell and Jones suffered injuries. So Woolen and Kelee Ringo had to take over.

"It was just hard," Woolen said. "I didn't play the whole second half. To go in the last three, four minutes, it's tough. The DB position is a mental position more than physicality. So to be able to check back in mentally when you've been out so long, it's hard. But I got to be there for my guys no matter what. No matter how I'm feeling. It's a team sport. No matter if I'm getting my way or not, I still gotta go play ball for my guys."

Woolen's teammates appreciated that he stayed focused and engaged even after he was pulled from the game.

"A hundred percent," Cooper DeJean said. "He's a heck of a player. Just to have the mental ability to go out and then be able to step back in when someone goes down, you really appreciate that. S—, he almost made a turnover for us. As a teammate, you definitely appreciate that."

Shortly after Woolen got back into the game, he was tested by a deep ball from Aaron Rodgers to Roman Wilson down the right sideline.

Woolen held up in coverage and the pass fell incomplete.

"It was good to see him respond there and then go out there and cover his guys in those situations," head coach Nick Sirianni said. "I know that's always tough when you come out of a football game, especially there at corner. But I've got a lot of faith in him and the things that he can do. We're going to need him moving forward."

The Eagles are probably going to need Woolen on Thanksgiving against the Cowboys. Mitchell suffered a hamstring injury on Sunday and Jones was seen after the game with a walking boot on his right foot.

So Woolen is likely going to go from benched to starting against a Cowboys offense that scored 51 points on Sunday.

Before the trade deadline, Woolen's name seemed to come up quite a bit but the Eagles didn't move him. Woolen on Sunday confirmed there were teams that called about him, but he was happy to stay in Philly.

Woolen signed a one-year, $12 million deal with the Eagles this offseason. It was viewed as a prove-it deal that could lead to a long-term contract going into 2027. But after 10 games, it's hard to determine what kind of value Woolen will have on the open market.

He can boost that market with a strong finish in 2026. Because despite getting taken out of Sunday's game, he's likely going to get more opportunities the rest of the way.

"Whenever you strive for greatness, everything ain't going to be easy," Woolen said. "You're going to have hardships. And I strive for greatness every day, even if I may fall short a couple times, I'm still going to keep going for greatness. That's how I'm able to continue to play through all the adversity."

#2697. Why France is at risk of recession — and what could keep it out of one

France's economy is flashing fresh recession warnings as businesses delay decisions ahead of the 2027 election — but resilient industries, progress on the budget and lower energy costs could help keep growth positive.

Business activity declined to a six-month low in November, with manufacturing performing worse than the services sector, according to the S&P Global Purchasing Managers' Index published on Monday.

The French private sector is at its most pessimistic about the coming year since late 2012, excluding the months at the start of the pandemic, according to the survey, with job losses recorded for a seventh straight month.

Among the biggest challenges cited by companies were economic conditions, uncertainty, competition and cost pressures, while frail domestic demand is weighing on sales.

France is heading toward a closely watched presidential election in April 2027, with polling suggesting voters may favor candidates offering a break from the policies associated with incumbent President Emmanuel Macron.

Politico polling this year has consistently indicated the front-runner is far-right candidate Marine Le Pen, who has recently made efforts to appease financial market jitters over France's fiscal sustainability, but has failed to convince many analysts that she can stabilize the country's debt trajectory.

The latest PMI figures add to a run of economic woes for France this year, including a dramatic rise in government borrowing costs, a surprise outlook downgrade by ratings agency Fitch, and a tumbling stock market which has underperformed its European peers.

This has compounded a sense of crisis after hundreds of thousands of people took to the streets in October and November to protest underfunding in education, the cost of living and the state of public services and housing.

The latest PMI survey has meanwhile raised the odds of France entering a technical recession — two consecutive prints of negative GDP growth — in the fourth quarter, according to Joe Hayes, senior principal economist at S&P Global Market Intelligence. The next print will follow a 0.1% contraction in the third quarter, though a revised Q3 reading is due on Friday.

"The eurozone's second-largest economy is struggling with weak domestic demand, in part due to hesitancy from clients to commit to projects ahead of a highly uncertain election period next year," Hayes said.

"We've subsequently seen a rapid deterioration in business confidence these past few months as other long-standing issues, such as concerns about France's fiscal position, resurface as the election draws closer."

"It's also notable that output price inflation has slowed sharply in November. This suggests that company pricing power has diminished and further highlights the fragility of the French economy," Hayes added.

Sharon Bell, senior European equity strategist at Goldman Sachs, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe" on Monday that most people had expected a bounce-back in France's PMI figures in Monday's release "on the basis of getting through the worst period of protests."

France's worst rioting in years in October not only racked up millions of dollars in insured losses but damaged the country's standing among some international investors, contributing to a sense of instability and an inability to pass reforms.

Bell said the gloomy PMI figures showed the weakness of French growth and its additional vulnerability alongside its election risk premium and large deficit.

A crucial factor for the French economy, according to Bell, will be whether a recent spike in European energy prices will begin to ease.

Sovereign risk has already started to become less of a distraction from solid European corporate fundamentals as energy pressures have started to let up over the last month, and will continue to do so if that continues, she told CNBC.

"If energy prices do stay high, then obviously that means higher inflation, higher rates, and more of a problem for the more vulnerable sovereigns. And France would definitely be in that group, probably the most important member of that group at the moment," she said.

Higher French government bond yields — stoked by a mix of its failure to pass a budget that will reduce the deficit, political paralysis and weak economic growth — have piled pressure on businesses as high rates spill over into corporate borrowing costs, making it more expensive to refinance debt or fund new projects.

French homebuilder Nexity said in an October trading update that a 10-year government bond yield near 5% had "reduced household borrowing capacity" as it recorded an 18% fall in sales to individual homebuyers in the first nine months.

Also in October, office landlord Gecina said it would be "very careful" about new capital spending amid pressure on its future financing costs, while bank Societe Generale said that the widening gap in French borrowing costs with Germany "could adversely affect the funding conditions of the Group and its clients."

Uncertainty around the outcome of the election, in which the far-left Jean-Luc Mélenchon is another popular candidate, is also frequently cited by analysts as deterring French business activity and investment.

Helene Gans, president and co-founder of investment fund Renaissance Capital, said in an opinion piece for Economie Matin earlier in November that companies are postponing decision-making, delaying near-finalized commitments and dragging out negotiations with a "worrying intensity" — attributing the fall in confidence to the run-up to the 2027 election.

However, Bank of France Governor Emmanuel Moulin told CNBC's Charlotte Reed in an exclusive interview last week that he does not forecast a recession, even if the central bank's current projection for 0.4% growth in the fourth quarter "might be a little bit too high."

"We don't see a recession coming, we see positive growth in Q4, this is looking at our now casting models," Moulin told CNBC.

"We have a survey every month of 8,500 companies, and we ask them whether their activity will grow in the next month, and there is a resilience of industry and of services in France, and in particular for industries and sectors that are doing well, like electricity, defense, aeronautics. These sectors are showing resilience and weathering the global shocks that we are seeing," he said.

Moulin nonetheless cited a lack of confidence and political uncertainty as key factors holding back economic performance this year.

"We are seeing ... worries about the situation of public finance, and this triggers high saving rates, worries about the future of pensions, and so all this tends to lower consumption, tends to be, you know, reducing investment," he said.

On borrowing costs, Moulin said spreads with nations such as Germany were "very high, and too high."

"They are too high compared with the situation of the French economy, its trends, but also its fiscal situation, which is not good, which needs to be dealt with, but which has not changed materially from, you know, June to September," he added.

"So there is some kind of, I think, overreaction of the markets. But I think it's important for the government to give signs that they are serious about reducing the deficit."

Moulin said that he was confident that the government's current budget proposal, designed to narrow the country's deficit from 5.4% of GDP to 5% of GDP, will be passed, even if it requires an executive decree or another measure if it fails to pass through the divided parliament.

French finance minister Roland Lescure said on Monday that the government's 2027 economic growth forecast of 1% was at risk of being downgraded, as he also backed the prospect of a budgetary "golden rule" — a reform that would impose stricter controls on public spending and would look to stop governments from running excessive deficits.

Le Pen proposed a golden rule in a recent speech, a concept that has also been backed by centrist candidate Edouard Philippe.

Moulin told CNBC last week that such a move would "enshrine the fiscal discipline in our institutions."

"Most of the [presidential] candidates have, you know, been quite affirmative in their resolution to reduce the deficit. All of them are making pleas... advocating that they will reduce the deficit down to 3%," he said.

"The consensus over reducing the deficit is broadening, but we don't need only words. We need acts, and the first act, I think, in order to show that the government is serious about fiscal discipline, is to have a budget."

#2696. Iran says three tankers hit mines in Hormuz despite U.S. escort, as Treasury widens sanctions

Iranian forces said three oil tankers caught fire after hitting naval mines in the Strait of Hormuz, the latest in a string of incidents in the waterway, as the U.S. escalates sanctions aimed at cutting off Tehran's financial lifelines.

In a statement carried by state news agency IRNA, Iran's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps said three "offending" oil tankers "suffered a severe explosion and fire after hitting naval mines" in the Strait of Hormuz.

The Guards said one tanker was near the southern coast and two were in the eastern part of the strait. All three were under extensive U.S. military air cover and escort that failed to prevent the explosions.

"U.S. military cover and escort will not replace compliance with maritime regulations and instructions from the IRGC Navy Traffic Control Center," the statement said.

Iranian forces have repeatedly warned commercial vessels that only the northern shipping lane has been approved by Tehran for safe passage, while attacking or threatening ships that use alternative routes near Oman.

The U.S. added a third aircraft carrier, the USS Theodore Roosevelt, to the Gulf last week, joining two other carriers in the fleet enforcing a naval blockade of Iranian ports.

The tanker incidents came after the U.S. Treasury on Monday announced new sanctions on more than two dozen Iranian exchange houses, front companies and key personnel as part of what it called "a sweeping effort to disrupt Iran's global shadow banking networks."

Iran's economy has come under growing pressure as Washington expanded sanctions under its so-called "Operation Economic Outcast" since August, aimed at crippling financial networks behind the country's oil revenue.

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent, in Monday's statement, described Iran's economy as "underwater," saying its shadow banking system was "the last financial lifeline of a bankrupt regime."

The conflict also appears to have extended to cyberspace, with Washington investigating Iran as a primary suspect behind dozens of cyberattacks targeting energy tankers, The Wall Street Journal reported Monday.

As many as 50 ships carrying oil, liquefied petroleum gas, chemicals and other potentially hazardous cargoes were believed to have been targeted, while some received warnings and bomb threats, the Journal reported, citing U.S. officials and others familiar with the matter.

U.S. authorities were also examining Russia as another possible culprit, according to the report, which described the widening attacks and occasional ransom messages as part of Iran's efforts to stoke fear among shipowners and further disrupt energy flows.

The foreign ministries of Iran and Russia did not respond to CNBC's requests for comment.

The conflict, triggered by the U.S. and Israeli strikes in late February, has seen Tehran close the Strait of Hormuz and the U.S. impose a blockade of Iranian ports in the Gulf, squeezing energy shipments through one of the world's most critical shipping conduits.

Oil futures inched lower Tuesday as investors looked past continued attacks on tankers to price in rising supplies from the region. On Tuesday, international benchmark Brent futures dipped to $89.2 a barrel, while U.S. West Texas Intermediate crude fell to $80.1 per barrel, down from the October high of nearly $105 per barrel.

Pressure on oil prices has eased as regional crude supply recovered and Russian exports rebounded, loosening supply constraints ahead of winter, said Gregory Brew, senior analyst for Iran and energy at Eurasia Group.

Russian refined-product exports recovered in November, with diesel shipments rising back to 400,000 barrels per day, Brew said. But "intensifying Iranian attacks in the Strait of Hormuz could reverse that trend," he added, estimating Brent would remain in the lower half of an $80-100-per-barrel range.

#2694. Eagles grades by position after win over Steelers

Eagles grades by position after win over Steelers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles snapped their seven-game losing streak on Sunday with a 19-16 win over the Pittsburgh Steelers.

You should probably hold off on booking your flight to the Super Bowl, but the Eagles improved to 3-7, winning their first game since late September.

Here are the position grades from this one:

Joe Milton: 11/21, 141 yards, 73.7 rating

There are plenty of throws from this game that Joe Milton is going to want back. He missed DeVonta Smith on a deep ball early in the third quarter that would have put this game away much earlier and he simply overthrew him. He also fired a ball high and wide to Smith on a big third down. He nearly threw a ball through Makai Lemon too. But Milton made some plays. His third-down pass to Smith to ice the game was a big-time play. He also had a couple explosives in the first half to Darius Cooper and Dallas Goedert. Milton has talent but he needs to find some more touch and consistency. Credit to him, though, on his first NFL win.

Saquon Barkley: 17 carries, 75 yards, 1 TD

It was the same old story for the Eagles rushing attack. It got cooking in the first half and disappeared in the second. Of Barkley's 75 yards, 59 came in the first half and 38 came on his long touchdown run. This was vintage Barkley.

Barkley ended up leaving this game with an injury so we'll see if he's able to rebound on a short week to play against the Cowboys on Thanksgiving. Will Shipley returned off IR and was the No. 2 in this game; he had 9 yards on 4 carries.

Darius Cooper: 4 catches on 5 targets, 59 yards

On third downs in this game, the Eagles kept turning to Darius Cooper. He led the team in receiving on Sunday afternoon. All four of his catches came on third downs and three of them moved the chains. Smith finished with 5 catches on 12 targets for 41 yards, but it wasn't his fault Milton overthrew him early in the third quarter. Smith had a couple of steps on his defender and would have had a 68-yard touchdown. Makai Lemon had just 1 catch on 3 targets for 3 yards.

Milton completed just 11 passes in this game so the stats aren't going to blow you away. Goedert's catch was a big one after he got loose on KJ Henry down the left sideline. That play helped set up the second Jake Elliott field goal. The Eagles asked their tight ends to stay in and help Markel Bell against T.J. Watt quite a bit in this game, but there was one penalty from Goedert (it was a weak call) and a few missed blocks from this group without much production.

It wasn't a perfect game but the Eagles' offensive line seemed to have a much better performance on Sunday than they have had in a while. The Eagles' rushing attack was able to get going at times and Milton definitely had more time against a defense that decided not to blitz very much. Jordan Mailata was beaten by Alex Highsmith for a sack. Highsmith had another one on a tackle-eligible play-action when John Ojukwu was supposed to block him. Markel Bell tackled T.J. Watt on a bullrush for a holding call but didn't give up a sack to the future Hall of Fame.

In their first game since the Jordan Davis trade, the Eagles' defense had their best performance against the run all season. They gave up just 73 rushing yards to the Steelers. Aaron Rodgers actually had more rushing yards (29) than starting running back Jaylen Warren (28). That run defense success started up front. But the Eagles also got after Rodgers for five sacks. Byron Young had 2, while Jonathan Greenard, Nolan Smith and Gabe Hall each had one. Jalyx Hunt had one taken away for a ticky-tack penalty on Quinyon Mitchell. The Eagles had 10 sacks in their first eight games; they have 11 in their last two.

The Eagles on Saturday placed Pro Bowl linebacker Zack Baun on Injured Reserve with a back injury so they started Jeremiah Trotter Jr. and Jihaad Campbell against the Steelers. Both had very good performances. Trotter led the team in tackles and continues to be really good at using his instincts in the run game. Campbell had the best performance of his season and was quicker to fire against the run. Even practice squad call-up Anfernee Orji got a tackle when Campbell briefly left the game.

Jonathan Jones: 5 tackles, 3 pass breakups

By the end of the game, Rodgers ended up completed 30 of 44 passes for 227 yards and a touchdown. The Eagles kept the Steelers out of the end zone until late in the fourth quarter when Kelee Ringo was on the field and gave up a 20-yard touchdown to Max Hurleman. The Eagles did an excellent job on DK Metcalf, who was eventually tossed when his emotions boiled over a second time. Metcalf was targeted 4 times and had just 1 catch for 2 yards. Quinyon Mitchell was on Metcalf for 14 plays and didn't give up a catch; Mitchell left early with a hamstring injury. Cooper DeJean played Metcalf on nine plays and gave up a two-yard catch.

The Eagles benched Riq Woolen in the first half after a couple missed tackles. His replacement, Jonathan Jones, had a key third-down PBU a few plays later. But because Jones and Mitchell got hurt, Woolen had to re-enter the game. He will likely need to start in a few days against the Cowboys.

Aside from the Jaydon Blue fumble on his kick return — and it was a costly error — the Eagles were excellent on special teams. Jake Elliott entered this game with just eight FG attempts all season but had four in this game and made all of them. That included kicks from 53 and 55 yards. That 55-yarder was crucial because it pushed the Eagles' lead to 10 points, which really mattered when the Steelers scored a touchdown on the ensuing drive. Elliott has also gotten better with his "dirty" kickoffs. And MVP Braden Mann had another excellent day. He had 4 punts for 237 yards (59.3) and downed 2 inside the 20. His best punt was a 70-yarder that pinned the Steelers at the 4.

The Eagles' coaching staff deserves credit. Sure, they turtled on offense in the second half but dialed up a gutsy pass on that third-down play to ice the game. And you can understand why Nick Sirianni went with a conservative approach with the way his defense was playing. Sirianni is now 6-0 coming off bye weeks in his time with the Eagles and that's probably not all a coincidence. Sean Mannion changed some things up in his second game with Milton as the QB and that seemed to help. Vic Fangio's group looked the way it was supposed to this season.

#2693. Signal joins United Airlines, banks in limiting AI agents over customer risks

Signal said AI agents built into mobile operating systems, such as Google's Gemini and Apple's Siri with onscreen awareness, can "scan" information on users' screens, potentially capturing and storing Signal data outside its control.

The company said it will block screenshots in its iOS app and, on Android, enable screen security when it detects an AI assistant attempting to read the screen.

Signal last year introduced similar restrictions against Microsoft's Recall AI on Windows 11. Its latest move adds to a broader push by companies including United Airlines, JPMorgan Chase and Charles Schwab to limit or block AI agents over security and for customer-protection concerns.

Whittaker said the new protections do not address every way an agent might gain access to data, including by retrieving content already stored on a user's device.

Signal also warned that third-party agents such as Meta's Muse could gain access to private app content through permissions granted during setup.

"Application-level protections will always have gaps, so the data an agent can reach has to be classified, permissioned and recoverable before the agent arrives," said Tianyi Jiang, CEO of data management and protection software company AvePoint.

AvePoint's State of AI 2026 report found that 88.4% of surveyed organizations had experienced at least one AI-agent-related security breach in the previous year. The survey covered 750 IT leaders globally.

"We continue to call on the major consumer operating system companies – Apple, Google and Microsoft – to provide simple technical tools and accurate documentation that give developers like Signal ways to prevent AI agents from accessing sensitive application data," said Whittaker.

Banks are also restricting third-party AI agents from accessing customer accounts.

JPMorgan Chase CEO Jamie Dimon said in October that outside agents were too risky for the bank and its clients. Morgan Stanley has blocked third-party agents from its platforms, Reuters reported.

Meanwhile, Charles Schwab CEO Richard Wurster said the brokerage blocks unauthorized agents to protect clients and the firm.

Singapore's OCBC Bank has said it will not let third-party agents to interact with the bank to transfer money autonomously and warned that "customers should exercise caution" when using such agents for banking.

United Airlines said Nov. 11 that it would restrict unauthorized agents from booking or managing tickets, citing risks that they could expose customer information, make unintended purchases or violate aviation rules.

"To book flights or do your grocery shop, an agent needs your email, accounts and payment details," said Mick Maleishko, an AI advocate at identity verification and compliance platform Sumsub.

He said that level of access creates a risk of account hijacking because actions taken by an agent may appear to a retailer or bank to have been authorized by the user.

Singapore Airlines, by contrast, is an early partner of Meta's Muse. Meta said users can ask the agent to explore travel, ticketing and reservation options through the airline.

"People treat agent risk as one-size-fits-all rather than matching the security needed to the actual task," said Yat Siu, executive chairman and co-founder of Animoca Brands. He said agents could help consumers act on their intentions and help businesses reach customers directly, but the risks vary by task.

"There's a much higher risk around an agent able to move meaningful sums of money or access sensitive personal data, than one making your restaurant reservations," Siu said. "The reality is that, just like internet applications to date, agents require tiered safeguards."

#2690. Jensen Huang is ‘too big to fire,’ putting Nvidia’s governance in focus: analysts

Jensen Huang's return as Nvidia CEO may have eased concerns over the chipmaker's leadership, but it has also brought renewed scrutiny to the company's corporate governance and its reliance on its founder.

Huang's abrupt departure and unexpected reinstatement exposed a deeper vulnerability at the heart of Nvidia, industry experts told CNBC, raising questions about board oversight and succession planning.

"What does it mean if you have a company too big to fail or a CEO too big to fire?" Angela Mancini, head of advisory for global risk insights at Dow Jones, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Tuesday.

Mancini pointed to Huang's unusually large number of direct reports as another sign of how heavily Nvidia depends on its founder.

"What does it mean when someone has 60 direct reports, and you literally are in a situation where you feel like you can't fire them?" she added.

For Nvidia, Huang's return offers an opportunity to address some of the governance issues brought to light by his departure.

Jeff Herbst, managing partner at GFT Ventures and a former Nvidia executive who spent nearly two decades at the company, said he had written to the board late last week calling for Huang's return.

While initially disappointed by the allegations surrounding Huang, Herbst said he came to view the episode as an opportunity for Nvidia to strengthen its governance, succession planning and overall management.

"While a lot of people saw a crisis here, I saw a potential opportunity," he said.

Herbst said Nvidia's management and oversight functions needed to evolve alongside its transformation from a relatively small technology company into one of the world's largest corporations.

"I think the corporate governance, the succession planning, the legal function, the regulatory function, the political function, all has to scale at the same time," he said.

He suggested Nvidia add more experience to its board by bringing in individuals who have run large companies and take a more formal approach to developing potential successors to Huang.

Tom Hulick, CEO of Strategy Asset Managers, agreed, saying the chip giant's "corporate governance definitely needs to be retooled."

It's "unheard of" and "unprecedented" for a president to enforce the reinstatement of a company's CEO, Hulick said on "Squawk Box Europe" on Tuesday.

"He's a born leader. He's enduring a lot of pain right now. Does he deserve to be back as CEO? It's not for me to speculate. Is he the right person? Probably for now."

However, Hulick added that the company still has "a lot of things to worry about" regarding corporate governance.

Huang's departure also exposed how closely Nvidia's leadership and long-term strategy are tied to its founder, raising questions about how the company would navigate a future transition.

Stacy Rasgon, managing director and senior U.S. semiconductor analyst at Bernstein Research, said the episode had revived attention to a risk investors had long overlooked.

"We all knew that there was key man risk here. We all chose, and I'm including myself in that. We all kind of chose to just ignore it," Rasgon told CNBC.

Succession planning, he said, is an issue both Huang and the board will need to address and communicate clearly to investors.

"I think both Jensen and the board will have to figure out, and I think communicate to investors," he added.

Herbst also pointed to Huang's personal role in driving Nvidia's technological innovations, including his willingness to invest in artificial intelligence and high-performance computing long before those businesses became profitable.

New markets such as autonomous systems and robotics would require major investment and a leader with Huang's passion and vision, Herbst added.

Correction: This story has been updated to reflect that Angela Mancini said Nvidia is in a situation where it feels like you can not fire the CEO.

#2689. The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats!

The 76ers are fun again, so it's the return of Roob's 76ers Stats! originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

This 76ers team is a heck of a lot of fun to watch and fun basketball means fun stats!

At 13-3, the 76ers are exactly a month into the 5th-best start in franchise history and best since they were 14-2 in 2000-01. Which turned out to be a pretty good year. They were 15-1 in 1949-50 and 1966-67 and 14-2 in 1981-82.

To help everybody get through this interminable two-day gap between 76ers games, here's our first 2026-27 installment of Roob's 76ers Stats, with a tip of the hat to Stathead.

→ The 76ers have been out-rebounded by 34 through 16 games (719-685) and their 13-3 record matches the best in NBA history by a team that's been out-rebounded after 16 games. Three other teams have been 13-3 despite getting out-rebounded, most recently the 2013-14 Miami Heat. The others are the 1979-80 Bucks and 2012-2013 Spurs. The 76ers are an NBA-best 6-3 this year when they've been out-rebounded (which means they're 7-0 when they have a rebounding edge).

→ In the win over Orlando Thursday, V.J. Edgecombe had 24 points, eight rebounds, six assists and two steals, and Joel Embiid had 26 points, six rebounds, seven assists and two steals. It was the first time two 76ers had at least 24 points, six rebounds and assists and two steals in a game in 53 years. In a win over the Celtics at the Spectrum in 1973, Fred Carter had 31 points, six rebounds, six assists and seven steals and George McGinniss had 28 points, 16 rebounds, eight assists and three steals.

→ Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, a career 37 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 44 percent so far. Anfernee Simons, a career 38 percent shooter from 3, is shooting 41 percent. Jabari Walker, a career 33 percent 3-point shooter, is at 42 percent. Dean Wade, a career 37 percent shooter, is at 41 percent. Jaylen Brown, a career 36 percent shooter from range, is at 39 percent. The 76ers are shooting just 36 percent as a team from the stripe because James and Embiid have been so bad, but everybody else is off to career-best starts from 3-point range.

→ The 76ers are 7-1 on the road, equalling their best road record ever after eight games. They were also 7-1 in 1980-81, 1981-82 and 1982-83. The 1981 team had the best road start in franchise history, going 12-1 before losing its second road game.

→ With 15.7 points, 6.8 assists and 6.8 rebounds per game, LeBron James is only the fifth 76er ever to average at least 15 ½ points and 6 ½ assists and rebounds through 16 games. The others are some familiar names: Wilt Chamberlain in 1967, Charles Barkley in 1987, Ben Simmons in 2018 and James Harden in 2023.

→ The 76ers already have more wins this year after trailing by 10 points through the first quarter than all but two full seasons since 1950. They had four in 1985-86 and again in 2022-23. The Hawks also have three wins this year when trailing by double digits after the first quarter, but they're 3-4 and the 76ers are 3-2. So they actually have a winning record when they're down by double digits going into the second quarter.

→ With Jaylen Brown (22.6, 6.6), Joel Embiid (20.0, 8.3), LeBron James (15.7, 6.8) and V.J. Edgecombe (13.6, 6.5), the 76ers are the only NBA team with four players averaging 6 ½ rebounds and 13 points. The last time they had four players in a season who averaged 13 points and 6 ½ rebounds was 1961-62 when they were the Syracuse Nationals, with Dave Gambee (16.7, 7.9), Hal Greer (22.8, 7.4), Red Kerr (16.3, 14.7), Dolph Schayes (14.7, 7.8) and Lee Shaffer (16.9, 6.8).

→ Edgecombe's 6.5 rebounds per game are 2nd-most among shooting guards behind only the Grizzlies' Cedric Coward, who is at 8.5. Edgecombe's 6.5 is also 2nd-highest ever by a 76ers shooting guard behind only Greer's 7.4 during that 1961-62 season. Edgecombe averaged 5.6 rebounds as a rookie and his 5.8 career average is highest by a shooting guard who spent more than one year with the 76ers. Greer is second at 5.3. Among all NBA players who've played at least 90 games, Edgecombe's 5.8 average is 11th-highest all-time among shooting guards (although Stathead includes some swing guard-forwards in their data).

→ Joel Embiid's 14.6 shooting percentage from 3 is worst in NBA history after 16 games (minimum 40 attempts). Embiid is 6-for-41 from the stripe this year. The previous 3-point shooting low 16 games into a season belonged to Cedric Ceballos of the Lakers in 1995-96 and the 76ers' Evan Turner in 2013-14. Both were 6-for-43 for 16.3 percent.

→ LeBron James is at 22.2 percent from 3 on 10-for-45. The Sixers are the first NBA team in 12 years with two players under 23 percent after 16 games with a minimum of 40 attempts. In 2014 Tony Wroten was 10-for-50 (10 percent) and Turner was 6-for-43 (16.3 percent).
