#2685. The biggest money-making energy trade looks over. What to buy instead

Is one of the greatest money-making trades of the last few years coming to an end?

No, I'm not talking about Nvidia or any of the data center- or AI-related power companies (although they have been crushed lately too… more on that on CNBC TV. Tune in!).

I'm talking about whether the epic boom in refinery-related stocks is unwinding. Over the last year, the jump in some of these companies has been nothing short of remarkable.

That jump is largely due to massive increases in margins, earnings, and cash flow. Those have occurred because of a big jump in what is called a crack spread. That is the difference between the price of oil and the finished price of refined products such as diesel and jet fuel. For most of this year, spreads have been soaring as disruptions and attacks on facilities and shipping in the Arabian Gulf, Hormuz, and deep inside Russia have played havoc with the market. In the last week or so, however, something big has changed: many of those spreads have started to tighten.

This seismic drop in spreads is causing a selloff in the aforementioned refinery companies.

If spreads continue to fall, these stocks may well keep selling off from recent highs. The upshot for investors may be that, as the shares come down, analysts see more upside. The median price target for Phillips 66 (PSX) now indicates a 20% gain from here, while smaller-cap Par Pacific (PARR) is double that.

Here are a few other key points to focus on heading into the Thanksgiving-shortened week.

I like to keep politics as much at arm's length as possible — we all know that energy and politics (sadly) often go hand in hand. Energy policies coming from Washington, D.C., can drive — or destroy — investment dollars. With all the big shifts in the White House over the last 10 years (four years of Trump, four years of Biden, and now 2 years into Trump #2), energy policies can blow through Washington faster than a summer squall hits a wind turbine. But because federal policies can mean so much for specific companies, sectors, and investments, it's important to follow the news flow.

That said, I'd like to take a moment to introduce you to the Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act, known as BAAJA (and pronounced like the state in Mexico). Like much legislation, BAAJA is an obscure but important bill working its way through the American Congress. BAAJA does a lot of things, but what Power Insider readers care about is its focus on energy permitting reform. In a nutshell, BAAJA would help speed up the building of new electricity infrastructure. Again, that's an overly simplistic view of a big bill, but it needs to be on your radar. The utility trade group Edison Electric Institute came out in favor of the legislation this month after the industry received some 11th-hour changes. See more about it in our recent interview with EEI President Drew Maloney here.

The bill passed the U.S. Senate in a vote late Thursday night. Now it moves to the House. Support for its final passage is also coming from the big LNG trade group, whose executive director said he "applauds the Senate for its bipartisan effort to pass meaningful, bipartisan, and durable permitting reforms aimed at delivering greater predictability, consistency, and certainty in the permitting, production, and export process for U.S. LNG. This legislation moves us closer to untangling red tape and eliminating overlapping agency reviews that can keep U.S. energy projects delayed for years."

The Natural Gas Supply Association also chimed in, saying it urges Congress to see the bill as "a resource-agnostic and balanced reform agenda for boosting American energy security. It keeps strong environmental reviews in place, but it draws a firm line against the endless procedural delays that tie critical energy projects in knots for a decade."

In my view, passing BAAJA is an important test of Congress's appetite for helping the energy and electrical power industry, lowering costs, and speeding time to market. The old adage was correct: time is money. Literally. If BAAJA moves on through the House, we could see an upside move in some utility stocks and any company related to energy infrastructure.

Speaking of politics, the news is getting lost in the massive Anthropic (ANT) IPO, but the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality (TCEQ) — whose importance I wrote about in the last edition — just decided to resume permitting for data centers. The Texas Tribune did a nice write-up on it, and you can read it here.

It's tough to say, but it's true. The two are inextricably linked by policies created at both the federal and state levels. As I wrote in the last edition, some federally elected officials may have less impact on state-level issues — like the data center buildout — but they certainly have power over national laws. While many of you are enthralled, disgusted, or confused by sordid tales of Nvidia and Jensen-gate, keep one eye open on what happens in Washington, D.C.

Have a great week, and have a wonderful Thanksgiving, everyone.

Perhaps the big investor story — which is, like most things lately, related to Jensen-gate and Nvidia—is the sizable selloff in many AI, neocloud, and data center power-related stocks.

Painful for current shareholders, but big drops can also bring big opportunities. Wall Street believes that's the case, at least right now. Look at some of the potential upside based on the stocks' prices versus their price targets.

We spoke with the CEO of Applied Digital (APLD) about all of this on CNBC last week, and you can watch it here. He highlighted how the market may be getting these stock stories wrong because, at least with his company, he has long-term contracts with solid-credit companies. In that way, Applied Digital is more like a landlord than a "pure-play" AI company.

My take → While I'm not going to recommend a specific stock, I would add that it is critical for investors to understand the differences between these companies. While they may have some things in common, they are also different, with different business models, customers, leases, and sources of revenue.

Speaking of recommendations… here are two ideas for you:

A recent decline presents a nice opportunity in SOLV Energy (MWH). UBS analyst Jon Windham upgrades SOLV to buy from neutral, believing the market is "underappreciating several key tailwinds for SOLV given uncertainty around data center demand and higher interest rates." UBS highlights how the stock has fallen lately despite making two deals and beating and raising recent guidance. UBS says now is an "attractive entry point" for SOLV. (Side note: MWH is a great stock ticker.)

While markets overall fall, BMO remains bullish on Bloom Energy (BE). After speaking with the company, the analyst is upgrading Bloom to outperform, saying he remains "increasingly confident of BE's commercial traction" and that he still has conviction even with the recent drop in shares. While analyst Ameet Thakkar did cut his target to $230 from $260, that's still 50% upside from the current price. Bloom shares have tumbled 45% since October 1.

I was recently asked for my opinion on the price of oil. Here's my take. The market clearly wants to push oil contract prices lower and has for a few months. But, as I've said and reported for a while, don't confuse the futures (paper) price with the actual price. Yes, shipping rates and insurance rates have come down from recent highs — and there is a good amount of oil now flowing through Hormuz — but the cost of a physical barrel of crude is still well above the futures contract. If we get real, longer-lasting peace around Iran, oil prices will fall. There is plenty of oil in the world. That said, if bombs and missiles start firing again, oil prices will firm. I know that's a non-answer answer, but the oil market is truly headline-driven right now. My late good friend Boone Pickens (later in life, he didn't like being called by the "T" in his first name) used to avoid giving a set price target and instead give a directional quote, so I'll do the same: the odds of oil being lower in two months than it is today are 75%.

Last week, I had a one-on-one with Vimal Kapur, chair and CEO of Honeywell International (HON), at an RBC conference in New York City. While the conversation wasn't filmed, I can tell you that Kapur offered investors reassurance that his order book is doing very, very well. This is important because Honeywell is huge in the things that make other things work. Its sensors go into a ton of energy facilities, from refineries to LNG facilities. Honeywell is a leading indicator of spending and buildouts, so if it keeps getting orders, it's a good sign for macro energy.

Bottom line: Watch Honeywell stock as a potential "tell."

This week's Inside Line interview takes a different approach. We hit our friend Craig Johnson up for actionable insights into energy and stocks. Craig is managing director and chief market technician at Piper Sandler. He recently appeared on Power Lunch to talk about markets and stocks. He mentioned being bullish on energy, so we wanted to dig in just a little bit more.

This week, we had the pleasure of chatting with Balaji Krishnamurthy, president of Chevron Australia. We spoke at length about natural gas, the impact of disruptions, and long-term demand from Chevron customers in the Asia-Pacific region.

This is a bit of a local RBI for the New York City area, but one I felt was important to highlight. New York's electric grid operator just came out with a massive warning for everyone in our region: in just a couple of years, there may not be enough electricity supply to power our needs. A new report shows a deficit in seven years, with a nearly 2 GW power hole in a decade. That's a number of big power plants' worth of energy. Get your solar panels on and buy that backup generator! I'll call this one random, but scary.

Catch up with more on energy including interviews and video content from CNBC and Power Insider.

#2682. Will Richard receives a postgame flopping fine

The NBA has issued a $2,000 fine to Golden State Warriors guard Will Richard for violating the league's anti-flopping rule during Golden State's dramatic 88-87 home victory over the Boston Celtics on Sunday night.

Richard, who logged 17 minutes off the bench and finished with three points, two rebounds, and two assists in the one-point thriller, was assessed the penalty following the league office's postgame review. Under the NBA's crackdown on embellishment, non-unsportsmanlike flopping violations carry a standard $2,000 fine.

Despite the minor setback to his wallet, the second-year guard out of Florida remains a key rotational piece for head coach Steve Kerr. Playing through a torn ligament in his left thumb earlier this month, Richard has continued to provide energetic perimeter defense and floor spacing, averaging 7.3 points and 2.3 rebounds per game while helping Golden State improve to 9-8 on the season.

#2681. 5 potential landing spots for Brandon Aiyuk after 49ers release

5 potential landing spots for Brandon Aiyuk after 49ers release originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Brandon Aiyuk era is over in San Francisco.

The 49ers on Monday made the decision to release him two weeks after his NFL reinstatement. His roster exemption was set to expire, with San Francisco opting to move on.

Aiyuk last played a game in October 2024, when he tore his ACL against the Kansas City Chiefs. A long-term series of off-field issues followed, and now the former 2020 first-round pick is on the market.

At his best, Aiyuk was one of the league's premier route runners. He steadily improved his production each season, peaking in 2023 with 1,342 receiving yards and seven touchdowns.

It's unclear how fit the 28-year-old wideout is to immediately impact a team right now, and if any team is willing to take the gamble given how the last few years have unfolded. But if he does find a new home, these five teams may make sense:

The Commanders have been the most rumored destination for quite some time, though, as aforementioned, it's unclear if any team is seriously interested. Aiyuk has a long-standing rapport with QB1 Jayden Daniels, which is an instant plus in terms of chemistry.

Washington is also down its best wideout in Terry McLaurin, who tore his left patellar tendon earlier in the season against…San Francisco. The Commanders are hoping to stay in the playoff race, but they need more talent beyond Stefon Diggs and Treylon Burks. Aiyuk could help.

Beyond Washington, Pittsburgh is another team that had some previous interest in bringing in Aiyuk. The Steelers have fallen to 6-4 after two straight losses, as their lack of offensive talent is bringing them down to earth following a solid start. Aaron Rodgers is the veteran starter under center, but DK Metcalf doesn't work anymore as the WR1.

Youngster Germie Bernard still needs time to become a reliable target, which probably won't happen with Rodgers around. So, if the Steelers want to fix some issues and bring in help to fix their sinking season, Aiyuk may be worth a gamble to add some flair in a tight AFC North.

Another team that could work is the Rams. Matthew Stafford and Co. are still a hopeful contender despite early-season woes. Those woes worsened with Puka Nacua now out for the year after MCL surgery, while veteran Davante Adams is battling knocks and isn't an out-and-out WR1 anymore.

In this scenario, Aiyuk gets to stay within the state he was born in and, perhaps most importantly, in the same division as San Francisco. Sean McVay runs a similar system to Kyle Shanahan, and Aiyuk may be able to provide important information in the NFC race.

Staying in the NFC West, the reigning Super Bowl champions could work, too. The offensive system isn't the same as Los Angeles', as Sam Darnold is a few tiers below Stafford from a pure signal-calling standpoint. But the Seahawks need more offensive weapons.

Jaxon Smith-Njigba does the heavy lifting in the passing game, having already accumulated over 1,200 receiving yards. But that isn't sustainable, with the next highest receiver being Cooper Kupp. He hasn't eclipsed 300 receiving yards. A healthy Aiyuk would round out a decent trio for Darnold.

What about the team that started this domino effect? Patrick Mahomes and Co. are 8-2 and back among the true AFC contenders, despite having a relatively poor offense. Travis Kelce is leading in receiving yards at age 37. That shouldn't have to be the case.

Rashee Rice and Xavier Worthy haven't impressed as much, while Kansas City acquired Josh Downs from the Indianapolis Colts at the deadline. But if they want to try to improve things further, Aiyuk in a Chiefs uniform would be an intriguing sight.

#2677. Eagles vs. Steelers predictions for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season

Eagles vs. Steelers predictions for Week 11 of the 2026 NFL season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-7) are back from their bye week and will host the Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) on Sunday afternoon at the Linc.

Someday this year the Eagles will win another game. I don't think it will be this one. The Steelers do a lot of things well. They're third in the NFL in run defense and seventh in scoring defense, allowing only 20 points per game, and T.J. Watt is still terrorizing quarterbacks at 32 years old. Undrafted Jaylen Warren leads the NFL at 5.7 yards per carry and is a dangerous receiver out of the backfield, future 1st-ballot Hall of Famer Aaron Rodgers can still make plays when he's protected – he's got 15 TD passes and six INTs this year – and D.J. Metcalf isn't what he used to be with Seattle but still capable of coming up big a few times a game. The Steelres are 6-3 and 4-1 in their last five and they don't have a ton of quality wins, but they aren't playing a quality team Sunday afternoon at the Linc. The Steelers aren't a Super Bowl threat, but they do a lot of things well. The Eagles don't do anything well. Except lose. They're good at that. Getting DeVonta Smith back and a healthier Saquon Barkley should help Joe Milton, but I just don't see the Eagles competing for 60 minutes with a solid team like the Steelers.

I don't think this Steelers team is as good as their record but I do think they're better than the Eagles. Even though the bye week should definitely help the Eagles find some better answers on offense as Joe Milton learns the scheme, that Steelers defense is good up front. There are some serious concerns about pass pro holding up in this game and the Steelers are hard to run against. The one advantage the Eagles have is at receiver as DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks return. But Milton will need time to throw. The Eagles' defense should be able to slow down this version of Aaron Rodgers but I just don't have a lot of faith that the Eagles will be able to snap their losing streak.

Last week's bye week was a much-needed break for this Eagles team. Players needed to heal up physically and mentally. As a player, the bye week allowed me to decompress and refocus on the season. Even when the season is going well, getting away helps the mind and body reset for the remainder of the season.

Coaches don't usually get that break. They try to figure out how to get the players performing better, fix what's going wrong and reinforce what they should continue doing right.

On offense, getting DeVonta Smith back will surely boost the passing attack for Joe Milton. Helping the O-line with protection should be the offense's No. 1 focus. Markel Bell will need help against one of the best pass rushers in the league in T.J. Watt. They must send a chipping RB, TE presence and slide the line toward him. Sean Mannion cannot let the right side of the line wreck the entire offense.

Defensively, Vic Fangio will have to call an aggressive style of play. The DBs will have to play tighter coverage or Aaron Rodgers will pick the defense apart. Stopping the run without Jordan Davis will also be a priority. A collective effort will be required to compensate for losing Davis on 1st and 2nd down. Can the Eagles keep the win streak going against the Steelers who have not won in Philadelphia since 1965? Maybe. Will they? Probably not.

Fresh off the bye week with some reinforcements on the way at receiver, you'd have to hope the Eagles offense shows some level of improvement. Against the Giants, they managed just one score and 30 of the 60 yards gained on that drive came via penalty. It has been an abysmal effort of late. Having DeVonta Smith and more time on task for new starting quarterback Joe Milton should — in theory — lead to better results.

Aaron Rodgers turns 43 next month and just lost his top interior protector on the offensive line in Mason McCormick. That should spell a big game for Jalen Carter. He tends to feast on inferior opponents. However, the Eagles are down both of their defensive captains. Zack Baun is dealing with a back injury and Jordan Davis was just dealt to the Jaguars. I suspect they'll struggle to stop the Steelers' rushing attack, led by Jaylen Warren.

The Steelers haven't won a game in Philadelphia since 1965. That changes on Sunday.

The Steelers are much better on paper than the way they are actually playing. Pittsburgh has a great defensive line… that doesn't get a lot of pressure. Talented receivers… to go along with one of the worst passing offenses in the NFL. The stats don't match up to the talent you see on the Steelers team. However, they are good at two things — running the football and winning close games. Two things the Eagles cannot seem to figure out so far this season. I am encouraged to see if the Eagles offense can make any strides with Joe Milton's second game at quarterback and the return of DeVonta Smith and Wicks. The Eagles' defensive line has a winnable matchup against a banged up Steelers offensive line but Aaron Rodgers is known for getting the ball out quick. I don't trust anything the Eagles are doing right now to be able to win this game.

It's good to have football back in the city of Philadelphia. I know it's been a frustrating campaign to say the least, but when football is in town, the city has a different type of energy to it. Against the Steelers, you know exactly the type of ballgame it's going to be. They want to play a slow, monotonous brand of football where they run the ball, churn clock, smother you defensively, and leave town with a win without doing anything spectacular. They're a veteran-laden unit headlined by future Hall of Famers in Aaron Rodgers and T.J. Watt, but it's their blend of youth-infused talent that showcases a well structured football team.

For the Eagles, I'm excited to see Joe Milton once again with what looks to be a WR core at full strength at time of writing. With extra time to prepare via the bye, I expect Milton to make some throws this week allowing his guys to make a few plays downfield. Pittsburgh is underwhelming in the secondary, and if Milton is granted just a few ticks of time to work through his progressions, the Eagles could create some explosive plays this week. Everything, however, starts with the offensive line, and I hope this coaching staff gives Markel Bell help against Watt or the longtime Steeler could have a handful of sacks by himself this weekend. That simply cannot happen. Double teams, chips, sliding protection, do something to help out your rookie RT whose confidence looks to have wavered mightily since the Jacksonville and Carolina games.

Defensively, limiting RB Jaylen Warren is the key to this week. He's the most underrated player in football in my book and the explosive engine that gets this whole thing going on offense for Pittsburgh. Limit him, and you have more than just a shot at winning this ballgame. My eyes are also on the edge rushers to create pressure on the soon-to-be 43 year old Rodgers, and the LB core which could be without Zack Baun. Shedding blocks with consistency and tackling in open space has lacked within this defense — that must improve on Sunday. Overall, I haven't seen this team adjust in months and this Steelers team is a rugged ballclub that just got embarrassed on Sunday Night football against the Bengals. Do I hope HC Nick Sirianni and OC Sean Mannion made adjustments during the bye week? Absolutely, but, my confidence in executing said changes is a whole other conversation.

#2674. Brooklyn Nets’ defense falls flat in 142-116 loss vs. Timberwolves

There's only so much that can go right on one end of the floor when nearly everything is going wrong on the other. Great defense means little if you can't score, and excellent offense doesn't matter if the defense is weak.

Brooklyn Nets learned that lesson again on Monday night against the Minnesota Timberwolves, 142-116, dropping them to 3-13 and marking their eighth double-digit loss of the season. It was yet another season-high for Brooklyn's opponent and fifth straight loss.

It didn't take long for that imbalance to show.

By the end of the first quarter, Brooklyn trailed 41-28. In his first career NBA start, following Nolan Traoré's hamstring injury, rookie Mikel Brown Jr. scored eight points in eight first-quarter minutes, shooting 3-of-5 from the field and hitting both 3-point attempts.

Julius Randle also dominated his former team, posting 29 points in 35 minutes along with four rebounds and four assists.

Entering the night as the league's 16th-ranked offense, Minnesota had its highest-scoring quarter of the season to start things off on Monday, dropping 41 points. It wasn't a particularly sluggish quarter for the Nets offensively; they scored 28 and hit an impressive 5-of-6 from deep.

However, defense was the biggest issue, allowing the Timberwolves to hit seven threes and win the fast-break points battle 10-0.

The story remained much the same in the second quarter. While the Nets tied the quarter, with 33 points apiece, the same exact issues prevailed. By the end of the first half, both teams were shooting over 50% from beyond the arc. The Nets still lost the fast-break points battle by a large margin (18-2), committed 12 turnovers against Minnesota's eight, and allowed 18 points off those compared to their 10. They were also dominated inside, allowing 30 points in the paint compared to their own 18.

Regardless of how connected the team's offense is, it's difficult to win games with that level of struggle on the defensive end of the floor. From the perimeter to the paint to the fast break, Brooklyn's defense simply couldn't find a groove in the first half. Monday also marked the fourth time in the past five games that the team has allowed over 70 points in the first half.

Their best defensive outing in that stretch came Friday, when they surrendered 62 to the Orlando Magic.

After the third quarter, however, the Timberwolves took a 115-91 lead. The tale of the game continued. Minnesota also finished the third quarter by scoring on 18 of their final 20 possessions. That just isn't a recipe for winning games, even though Brooklyn shot 13-of-22 from 3-point range through three quarters.

It wasn't all ugly. There are certainly some individual performances to highlight.

In his first start, Brown Jr. impressed with 17 points in 29 minutes, coupled with five rebounds and seven assists. He led the team in first-half points but didn't score in the second half. The gripe was his turnovers, logging six against seven assists.

In his second straight start over Egor Dëmin, Danny Wolf also provided a meaningful all-around presence for Brooklyn. Wolf compiled 11 points, five boards, and four assists in 25 minutes, including a 3-of-5 mark from downtown.

At the end of the day, though, until the Nets find some semblance of defensive consistency, even their excellent offensive outings will be hard to turn into wins.

It hasn't been the easiest beginning for Mikel Brown Jr. After all, how could it be? He's been on on a minutes restriction to begin his career. He had himself a solid outing on Monday night in front of a national crowd.

Check out his exclusive with Austin Rivers:

The Nets are off Tuesday, before traveling to face the worst team in basketball: the Milwaukee Bucks, with a 1-16 record. Tip-off is set for 8:00 p.m. ET. Now sitting at 3-13, the Nets have no freebies on the schedule, but Wednesday is a highly winnable game for the team.

#2671. Nvidia surge: What investors should do with the stock and the AI trade now

Nvidia shares gapped higher at the open by more than 30%, on pace for its biggest one-day gain in 23 years on Monday, following the news just before the bell that former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang is going to be reinstated.

Investors and traders said that while this reflex rally may have more to run, cautiousness is warranted on Nvidia and the AI trade because of the overhang of investigations into the alleged Chinese espionage involving the company and others, as well as the possibility for data center moratoriums as Democrats take control of Congress and key governor posts in January.

"Am I as bullish on Nvidia now as I was on Friday? Of course not, you can't be," said Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management, which counted Nvidia as its largest position as he bought on the dip over the last few weeks. "I'm trimming back."

"All the same issues that were here before Jensen resigned are still here," he added.

The day before Huang resigned on Nov. 12 as CEO, Nvidia closed at $173.73. It's intraday high on Monday was $171.50, up from its recent low around $125 as it was selling off in the wake controversies. The VanEck Semiconductor ETF closed at $506.96 the day before his resignation. It reached a high of $469.35 before losing steam as trading wore on Monday.

Mike Khouw, YieldMax strategist at Tidal Financial Group, told CNBC on Monday that there were "sighs of relief," "applause" and even "cheering" among investors at the news of Huang's potential reinstatement. But he also said there are still considerable risks facing the AI trade.

Jensen Huang was not the only problem at Nvidia, he said, and there are still many vulnerabilities in the overall AI trade, which should give investors pause. Credit-sensitive companies are still at risk of faltering, Khouw added.

The national security implications of the scandal that has rocked Nvidia and the global AI trade more broadly aren't going away any time soon even as the Trump administration assured the Nvidia board there were none. Investors on Wall Street are expressing strategic concerns about ways to play the tech space just as lawmakers in Washington are demanding answers.

Democrats are demanding answers on what exactly happened with Nvidia and the Chinese government.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-Mass.) said in a Monday statement. "The Trump Administration also needs to explain to Congress what 'input' was given and by which senior officials in the United States government to support his reinstatement."

House Ways and Means ranking member Richard Neal (D-Mass.) stressed the importance of Congressional oversight in determining the best course of action to move forward. The outcome of any Congressional investigations will likely be crucial for Nvidia's long-term viability as an investment opportunity, especially since the company does so much international business.

"This is an export control issue," Neal told CNBC on Monday. "Congress is not subservient to this president or any other president as it relates to trade and tariff policies. This is a trade related issue."

Many of the same Democrats will call for a slowdown in the data center buildout, a strong issue for the public partly responsible for their Blue Wave earlier this month.

"You're going to have to work your way through what these data center moratoriums all mean," said Niles. "Now I think the broader market keeps going but I am not as convinced on the move in the data-center centric names from here."

To be sure, many Wall Street analysts were rushing to upgrade Nvidia's stock on Monday after Huang's reinstatement. Most restored price targets well above $200 with one as high as $270. But many traders think the rebound may fail before getting back to those levels.

Famed short seller Michael Burry, who made a boatload betting against Nvidia with put options as it cratered, told his Substack audience that he thinks the rebound here could be "outsized," especially with low volume in the market overall because of the Thanksgiving holiday this week.

But he too sees the rally eventually losing steam.

"Jensen being back without much more information might even restore NVDA to the high 100s," Burry wrote. "I won't short it on this information I have today until it gets closer to or maybe hurdles $200."

#2670. Sixers Bell Ringer: Sixers undone by Portland’s blazing hot shooting

Sixers Bell Ringer Season Standings:VJ Edgecombe – 4Jaylen Brown – 3.5Tyrese Maxey – 3.5Joel Embiid – 3LeBron James – 1Anfernee Simons – 1Dean Wade – 1

The Philadelphia 76ers simply could not stop the red hot hands of the Portland Trail Blazers in a 128-111 loss on Monday night.

Joel Embiid did end up missing this one due to illness after spending the day as questionable on the injury report. Jabari Walker started in his absence. The Sixers were otherwise healthy.

This was a shootout early with some hot shooting from both teams in the first half. The Sixers hit 57.8% from the floor and 37.5% from long range, with the Trail Blazers hitting 50.0% and 46.3% respectively. It all culminated in a LeBron James buzzer-beating three to end the half and knot things at 69-69 going into the break.

The Trail Blazers, unfortunately, remained red hot from long range. It was a dual problem: partially due to the Sixers' help defense leaving way too much room for guys on the perimeter, and partially due to Portland just knocking down absolutely everything. They continued firing as the Sixers went cold, building a double-digit lead early in the final frame. Shooting regression certainly had hit Philadelphia… but Portland seemed immune. The Sixers couldn't hit a damn thing and the Trail Blazers were able to pull away fairly comfortably.

The Sixers are now 14-4 on the season as their win streak has been cut short at five games. They're off Tuesday and will be back on Wednesday evening for a Thanksgiving Eve game.

Until then, let's get to the Bell Ringer. Most of these candidates are here for their first half effort more so than the rough, cold second half that affected pretty much everyone in a Sixers uniform.

Lebron James: 23 points (8-for-13 field goal, 4-for-5 3PT shooting), 6 rebounds, 3 assists

(Photo by David Dow/NBAE via Getty Images)

Especially in the absence of Joel Embiid, there was extra work on LeBron James shoulders to really be the engine of the Sixers' offense. Of course he was up to the challenge, he's the King. After a first half of efficient shooting and some patented incredible dimes to teammates, LeBron had 16 points on 6-for-8 field goal, 3-for-3 three-point shooting with three assists going into the break. Those three triples included a buzzer beater to tie the game at the end of the second frame. He suffered from the same cold stagnant streak that hit the rest of the Sixers in the second half, but it was an incredible few periods from LeBron.

James finished with 23 points on 8-for-13 field goal, 4-for-5 three-point shooting with six rebounds and three assists.

Jaylen Brown: 20 points, 2 rebounds, 4 assists, 2 steals

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images)

Jaylen Brown just makes it look so easy sometimes. And he made it look easy on a night returning from a few games off due to a hamstring strain. Whether it was being in the right place at the right time or working isolation to create shot opportunities out of nothing, Brown looked healthy and ready to work tonight. By halftime, Brown was leading the Sixers with 19 points on 8-for-10 field goal, 1-for-2 three-point shooting with three assists. He unfortunately picked up his fourth foul early in the third which sent him to the bench and, to be honest, the Sixers' offensive momentum took a seat with him. That was the beginning of the end for the Sixers. Really illustrates how critical Brown can be to this team's offensive groove.

Brown finished with 20 points on 8-for-14 field goal shooting with two rebounds, four assists and two steals.

Tyrese Maxey: 22 points, 2 rebounds, 5 assists

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

It was far from Tyrese Maxey's best night, I'll be honest. He had some inconsistencies shooting the ball so he couldn't really get going for any of the one-man streaks he tends to string together. But he wasn't discouraged, and instead kept waiting for better opportunities to come and chipped in points where he could get them to fall. This was especially true in the third period — critical, as everyone else for the Sixers went relatively cold during that frame — where he knocked in a a number of big buckets to keep the Trail Blazers close as they tried to pull away. After scoring just 12 in the first half, Maxey added 10 points in the third to give the Sixers a fighting chance. They didn't take advantage of that by any means, but they still had a chance.

It was an up and down night, but Maxey ended up one of the Sixers' best scorers — 22 points on the night with two rebounds and five assists.

#2669. Eagles place Zack Baun, Dontayvion Wicks on IR in roster flurry

Eagles place Zack Baun, Dontayvion Wicks on IR in roster flurry originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles on Saturday afternoon placed LB Zack Baun (back) and WR Dontayvion Wicks (ankle/foot) on Injured Reserve in a flurry of roster moves.

They will miss at least the next four games.

Before Campbell missed a couple games with injuries of his own, those two split reps next to Baun. This will give the Eagles an extended look at both of those young 'backers.

Baun, 29, was playing at a very high level again this season. The two-time Pro Bowler had 65 tackles, 2 sacks, 8 TFLs, 5 QB hits and 5 pass breakups in 8 games this season.

Wicks, 25, missed the three games before the bye week with an ankle injury but was able to return to practice this week. He then was ruled out for this game on Friday as the foot injury was added to the report. At least DeVonta Smith (hamstring) will return this weekend for the Birds.

In addition to placing those two players on IR, the Eagles made the following roster moves on Saturday:

• RB Will Shipley (foot) was activated off Injured Reserve, where he had been since Oct. 16. Shipley was able to return to practice this week. He joins Saquon Barkley and Jaydon Blue as the running backs on the roster.

Expect Shipley to slot in between Barkley and Blue in the pecking order. He'll also take a significant role on special teams. Shipley will retake his role as a kick returner.

• Both DT Gabe Hall and LB Anfernee Orji were elevated for the Steelers game.

Hall will be the fifth defensive tackle after the Eagles traded Jordan Davis ahead of the deadline. He's behind Jalen Carter, Moro Ojomo, Byron Young and Ty Robinson. Sunday will be Hall's 2026 debut; he played in two games in 2025.

Orji first joined the Eagles' practice squad in early October. This is his second stint with the Eagles. He hasn't played in a regular season game since 2024 with the Saints.

#2668. Bengals’ Zac Taylor Explains Decision That Backfired in Loss to Commanders

Cincinnati Bengals head coach Zac Taylor detailed what the plan was in the final minutes of the game in the loss to the Washington Commanders. Coming out of the two-minute warning, Joe Burrow completed a 32-yard pass to Mike Gesicki to get the Commanders' 33-yard line.

After that play, it all went downhill for the Bengals as Cincinnati decided to focus on getting the first down rather than trying to kill as much of the game clock and then set up the field goal.

Nonetheless, Taylor and his team failed to secure the first down, which included a play in which Burrow was almost intercepted by Rasul Douglas. After the loss, Taylor spoke to the media to discuss his decision-making process on that drive as Cincinnati went ahead 20-19, but alloweing 46 second on the clock.

"We were trying to get the first down," Taylor told reporters after the Bengals 22-20 loss to the Commanders on Nov. 23. "That was our biggest takeaway. Obviously, they had to use a timeout because of an injury, which they weren't planning on doing.

"But we wanted to throw the ball and get the first down so that we could end it with a field goal and they wouldn't get the option to get the ball back. But I think that's just overall, you run it twice, and then you're disappointed you're in a fourth-and-9 and kicking a field goal.

"They're going to get the ball back with whatever it was, 50 seconds left, not counting that injury timeout because you can't anticipate that. But that was the mindset there."

Meanwhile, Taylor spoke about the offensive performance as Burrow and Co. were only able to put up 20 points while recording 312 total yards.

" I just thought we didn't start very well," Taylor added. "They're a very aggressive defense, so it's going to be a little clunky at times when they find some momentum, which they did early in the game. They came after us, and we didn't do a great job providing the answers until about that third or fourth drive.

"Then we found some momentum. We started on the minus-10, drove it all the way down the field, kicked a field goal, went down and scored a touchdown, and scored another touchdown to start the second half. I felt like we had found some momentum there and just didn't finish it the way we wanted to in the second half."

Moreover, Taylor gave credit to the Commanders for their performance as they took advantage of his offense not finishing drives with touchdowns.

"If we get a stop on that last drive, I'm feeling pretty good about it," Taylor said. "Their quarterback is a really explosive player. He can extend a lot of plays. We saw he can make plays with his feet. He can make any throw on the field, so he's very dangerous. This is a good football team.

"We've watched these last five games as they took the Niners down to the wire. They took the Rams down to the wire, and they got the two wins they got, and then they tied one. So we knew what we were dealing with when we came here, and they got the best of us today."

#2664. Mr. Rocket finally hits his target to ice a much-needed win

Mr. Rocket finally hits his target to ice a much-needed win originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Joe Milton missed a bunch of throws on Sunday. It was far from a perfect game against the Steelers in his second-career NFL start.

But with the game on the line, Milton came through in the Eagles' 19-16 win.

With just 1:09 remaining, the Eagles were facing a 3rd-and-6 from their own 39-yard line. They could have run the ball again, forced the Steelers to use their final timeout, and punted the ball away. Instead, Nick Sirianni got aggressive and put the ball in Milton's hands.

Milton said it meant a lot that the coaching staff trusted him in that situation. He repaid that trust when he hit DeVonta Smith with a perfect pass for a seven-yard gain to ice a win that snapped a seven-game losing streak.

"I told [Smith] in the huddle that I'm going to come back (to him)," Milton said. "Just keep running your routes hard. Those small things that he's coming back this week, just small things that we have to clean up and get better at and get more dialed in. That just takes more practice and reps, just completing the ball to him and getting him a friendly football."

Before that completion, Milton had completed just 10 of 20 passes in the game as the Eagles' offense completely dried up in the second half. To make matters worse, Milton missed several throws that he really needs to make.

But there's something special about a guy who can keep his confidence after mistakes and can make a play like that after a less-than-stellar game. It's something Milton has struggled to do at times.

"Every play you go through out there, that's the only play that matters," Milton said. "Once that play is over with, you have to know how to flush it. Like I talked about previously, I've been through a lot of adversity. I've been in a lot of situations where you put me in that situation six years ago, and I probably would've still been thinking about the [previous] play and not being able to flush it. Being in the moment now, I'm being where my feet are, I'm able to flush the play and move on with the next one."

Milton is known for his strong right arm — his nickname is Mr. Rocket — and we saw that again on Sunday. Sometimes that was good, sometimes that was bad.

In the first half, Milton was able to scramble to his left before throwing a cross-body pass on a rope to Darius Cooper for a 29-yard gain on 3rd-and-13. But there were other plays where Milton couldn't harness his velocity.

On one play, he nearly threw a ball through Makai Lemon. And on the Eagles' first offensive play of the second half, he overthrew DeVonta Smith on what would have been a 68-yard touchdown.

"On throws like that, I usually say my daughter's name because it helps me relax," Milton said. "I didn't at that time, obviously. … I saw it, and I just got real excited because before that play I just told him we're gonna go home. It's time to go home. It's time to go score. When I've got the look that I wanted, I just got overly excited. But yeah, you just got to hit those, and especially in those moments."

Milton says he really does sometimes say his daughter's name — Maleia was born on Oct. 9, 2025 — before unleashing a throw. It helps him relax.

"Basically it kind of tells [me] in my mind that I'm throwing the ball to her, so it can't be as hard," Milton said. "Show a little love."

The Eagles are now 3-7 as they head to Dallas to face the Cowboys on Thanksgiving. The Eagles signed Milton off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16. A little over a month later, he's going to start against them.

The Cowboys scored 51 points on Sunday, so if the Eagles are going to win, they're probably going to need Milton to show off that arm again in a few days.

And if you needed any reminder that Milton has a strong arm, he was wearing a rocket pendant around his neck after his first NFL win as a starter. He bought that chain for himself when he went to the Combine a couple years ago.

"Yeah, that's me, Mr. Rocket," Milton said. "It's on my wall in my game room. That's me, that's my name. I love it. I'm blessed to be able to throw the ball far, and however strong my arm is. Yeah, Mr. Rocket, I love it. It's me."

#2661. NASCAR Driver Makes First Public Appearance After Scary Daytona Crash

Frankie Muniz has made his first public appearance since his scary crash at Daytona International Speedway, showing up at the Las Vegas Grand Prix just 10 days after the incident. The NASCAR driver was spotted in the Formula 1 paddock on Saturday, marking his first documented outing since the November 11 practice crash.

Muniz, who drives the No. 33 Ford for Team Reaume in the NASCAR Craftsman Truck Series, appeared at the Las Vegas Strip Circuit wearing a Ford Racing jacket and cap. He was scheduled to participate in Pirelli Hot Laps following the opening practice session.

His appearance came as he continues to recover from the Daytona accident, which left him sore but without any major injuries. Although he has not announced a return date to racing, the Las Vegas sighting offered a new update on his recovery.

Muniz was involved in a crash on November 11 during practice for the Ford's Proud to Honor Veterans Day Classic at Daytona International Speedway. The exhibition event supported military and veteran charities, and Muniz was scheduled to drive a Mustang Dark Horse R for Team Air Force alongside Austin Cindric.

Muniz crashed in the infield section of the road course. Safety crews had to remove the roof of the car to get him out before transporting him to a local hospital for evaluation. Doctors diagnosed him with soreness, and he was released later that day. He did not compete in the exhibition race.

Following the Daytona crash, Muniz said he was "extremely sore" but had "no major injuries." He thanked Ford Racing, Daytona International Speedway, hospital staff and the safety crews for their help.

"I'm grateful to be walking right now," he said in a video update. "I'll be back on the track soon."

Ford confirmed that Muniz was awake and alert after the crash, while NASCAR also confirmed that he had been released from the hospital. Austin Cindric ultimately won the exhibition race, with a teammate taking Muniz's place in the event.

Muniz had a busy 2026 racing season, competing full-time in the NASCAR Craftsman Truck Series and also racing in the SRO GT4 America series.

He partnered with Tyler Stone to win the GT4 America Am championship, driving a Ford Mustang GT4. His Daytona crash came near the end of an active racing campaign.

The former Malcolm in the Middle star has gradually built a racing career since stepping away from acting. He has made more than 100 Truck Series starts in recent seasons and continues to compete in stock car racing.

His appearance at the Las Vegas Grand Prix does not confirm a return to competition or medical clearance. However, Muniz remains involved with Ford's motorsports activities, having previously taken part in Ford Performance hot laps at the same circuit in 2024 and 2025.

He has not provided a specific timeline for his NASCAR return, and no additional medical details have been released. Fans will be watching for his next update as he prepares for the 2027 racing season.

#2656. Cavs lose 122-108 to Bulls after walloping in first half

The Cleveland Cavaliers lost 122-108 to the Chicago Bulls after taking a beating in the first half. Donovan Mitchell led the team with 28 points.

The second quarter was an outright disaster. Chicago opened up with a 19-6 run in the first four minutes, and would extend that to 32-10 on their way to a dominant lead.

Cleveland entered tonight as the 10th-worst transition defense in the league. It showed.

The Bulls broke free in transition over and over again in the second quarter. The Cavs couldn't keep up or slow them down, and Chicago reaped the rewards. Everything from poor effort to bad communication contributed to this. But more than anything, the Cavs couldn't score the ball to regain control of the pace. Missed shots and turnovers led to more fast-break opportunities for the opponent.

That wasn't all. Chicago's bench opened the game by outscoring Cleveland 48-9 in the first half. The first quarter was mostly fine, but the second quarter was a running rampage from the Bulls.

The Cavs trailed by 22 points going into halftime.

It was an inspiring start to the third quarter that gave the Cavs life. They jumped out to a quick start, chipping away at the Bulls by working downhill into the paint and playing with an advantage. Converting those opportunities helped them regain control of the game's pace.

James Harden was instrumental in the Cavs' run. He probed into the paint and either scored, dished an assist, or drew a foul throughout the third quarter. Harden scored 11 of his 21 points in the third quarter.

Cleveland would eventually get the game to within two possessions, but fell back down by double digits once the Bulls reignited their flame in the fourth quarter.

It was a strong second half from the Cavs, but they gave themselves too thin a margin of error to complete the comeback. That's what happens when you lose the second quarter by 25 points. Not to mention, the Bulls took 17 more shot attempts than Cleveland.

Jordan Hawkins lit it up for 19 points off the bench on 7-for-13 shooting. It was a confident performance from a younger player who is trying to establish himself in the NBA. The Cavs certainly won't forget him the next time they play Hawkins.

Cleveland falls to 9-9 on the season and will play the New York Knicks on Wednesday.
